2. **The eleventh question is *what are they called* and the answer given to it on the fiftieth day of the new month was four words long, was not a noun, and was *nobody has asked them*.** It was given by the woman who wrote the question, in daylight, in a converted salt store, in about two hundred and forty people, and it is entered **in a column, on the same page as the question and not on a separate sheet, and read back twice in two languages.** **The moment a review answers that question with a description the licence is gone; the moment it answers it with a refusal the licence survives and is weaker, because a review survived by a refusal is a review that has written down that this city refuses.** The answer had been on the front of a slate for three days while the question it answered was on the back, and nobody turned it over, because nobody had to.

3. **A rule that covers a document is a rule about a document, and the volume's midpoint failure is this sentence turned over and used on the man who wrote it.** Chapter 0079. The bar on Adrian Vale is **perfect, is not broken, and did not reach**; a pale field in a room is not the instrument; **nobody used anything and there was no hand on any lever.** The thing it did not reach was a person, on a resonant place with a boundary cut into it, in the wrong weather. **A later chapter may not soften this, may not make it somebody else's fault, and may not call the bar a mistake.** A bar on a document is a bar and not a wall, and this series now has two proven instances of the same rule on the page: a pale field stopped by a page read aloud in nine seconds, and a page that did not reach a person at all.

4. **A workway is for a promise.** Chapter 0078, and no character in this series had said it out loud in seventy-eight days. A workway is a permission profile for **one direct Veyran promise**, not a strength and not a hand. **A person who answers a promise that was not made to her gets a pattern and a hand and no witness and no cost, and is therefore a hold, and the residue of a hold goes into the person who did it.** Mina Okoye's sentence, and it is hers alone, and it was said by her about a person who is not her, and nobody claimed it. **A person who is standing in a working and has not been asked has not agreed to it and is therefore not a witness** — this is now the standing test for whether a scene contains a working at all.

5. **There is no office in Saltwake for a hand that holds things, and the field that would create one is the field that arrived in the same hour as the consequence of there not being one.** Chapter 0078. **Nobody accepted it and nobody refused it, and it is the first field in the series that no character has answered at all.** A later writer may not have a character accept a field without the book first having asked the person whether she meant it, and may not treat a refusal of a field as a gain, because the refusal is not the interesting half.

6. **The right to be asked is a thing a holder can refuse, and a page can remove the asking and thereby remove the only question anybody could have asked her.** Chapters 0073 and 0078. The twentieth field said a holder's work may be described without anybody asking her first and that **nobody may be asked to describe it**; read against the first line, it is a licence to be written about, and in Chapter 0078 the consequence was written on the back of it in four hands: **the only instrument in this city that could have asked her what she did is the one that took the asking away.** Any later payment, remedy or instrument in this series that treats a holder's consent as already given is this mechanism again.

7. **A description is the only thing in this world that has never cost anybody anything until an office reads it.** Chapter 0076, Adrian's, thirty-two words, and it is **not entered in the book because it is a finding and findings may not be entered by the man it is about.** This is the volume's general form of the rule and it applies to the form on the nail, the roll of strangers, the schedule of crossings, the new line that may be asked once, and the page Adrian entered on the Reed Reach charge.

8. **A person may be asked whether he is able to do a thing and the answer may also be *I can do some and not others*, and the answer is entered.** Chapter 0074. The nineteenth line of the nineteen was **amended for the worse, at the asking of the man it is about, in daylight, in about two hundred and forty people** — the first time a line on the keeper's board has been changed by the person the line is about. **A working can be made cheaper without being made private** and can be made more accurate without being made private either, and neither of those is the same as being made private.

9. **A man who is asked and says no is not a man who did not turn up.** Chapter 0074, and in seventy-four days of the instrument those had been the same man every time. **Nobody thanked the man who said no and nobody thanked the man who asked**, and that is entered as a fact and not as a complaint.

10. **A prediction about a fraction of a number that is not the number is not a prediction, and a prediction which is wrong in the direction it wanted to be is worse than one that is wrong the other way.** Chapter 0074, Renna Ord's, twenty-two words. The roll of the forty is thirty-eight. **A later writer may not round, may not reconcile the number with the prediction, and may not use the four as a rate.**

11. **The first line added to an instrument for the benefit of a person who is not a witness is bounded, dated and refusable, and the date is the whole of it.** Chapter 0076. *The person this instrument costs may be asked, once, on a named day, in daylight, in a room, and the answer may be no; the refusal is entered; the asking is not repeated on a second day; the person is not entered on any roll by having been asked; the answer is not a fault and is not a wage and does not go in the roll.* **A line with no date in it is the old form of the oath, and a standing cannot be refused.** A later writer may not add a second asking to the same person on the same ground, and may not put the asking on a slate, because **a question that is put on a slate is a question asked of everybody on every day, and a question asked of everybody on every day is a form.**

12. **A statement of error may be answered in one of three ways and the third clause is a real procedure.** Chapter 0079. **The person named may answer in public within a stated term, may withdraw it, or may say publicly that they stand by it. The third is the only one that requires no sentence, and it is the one Adrian Vale used in a yard in front of about two hundred and forty people, and it was correct, and nobody may write a chapter in which he answers a statement of error with a speech.** A statement of error stands unless the person who entered it withdraws it, and no one except that person may remove it.

13. **A rule that only covers the thing you can see is a rule about a thing and not about the world; a standing that cannot be refused; a guild that does not go home.** **All three are now on the page about the man who wrote the page**, in that order of weight, in a yard, in about two hundred and forty people, in Chapter 0079, and the third is the one that lands. **The name Single Witness and the name First Grammar are still not used in prose and must not be used before Chapter 0091, in a room, in daylight, with a date on it.** A later writer may not collapse the three and may not name them.

14. **There is nothing here to resign from, and a man with nothing to resign is not a man being let go.** Chapter 0079. Nineteen words in a notary's hand. **A vacant seat is not a fault, a liability is a post with a person in it, and a person who holds no office cannot be removed from one; any later chapter that wants Adrian Vale to be relieved of something must first give him something.**

15. **A shrine is a record, and a record that cannot be removed, and a page can be put inside one.** Chapter 0080. **It is the first page in this series put somewhere that is not this city's building, it carries the six things of the Wayhouse with a stone in the middle and a household in it, the price is written under the six and is not a seventh, and the keeping household may take the page down on a date and is not asked why twice.** **A page with no price on it is a favour and a favour is a thing that stops.** Nobody stepped over it. A person may refuse to have her name on a page, and the reason given was eleven words, and the reason is a rule.

16. **A description is a removal with a column for where the person is, and a page that asks is not a roll and a page that names is.** Chapter 0079. Adrian's page on the Reed Reach charge asks and names nobody, **and the Reed Commons refused it in about nineteen minutes, because a page that asks four people is a description, and in four months a man in a building nine hundred miles away will read four names off the back of it.** The fourth line of the term sheet is in force and the whole of the charge is still on one man. **The refusal was correct and is on the page and was not softened.**

17. **An incomplete return is a real procedure, and the procedure is a hearing, and a hearing is a date.** Chapter 0077. A Crown office that cannot fill a column sends a form back and gets a date, and the district that writes *nobody has been asked and none has agreed and this is not the same as none existing* into a cell is the first body in ninety years that its own office cannot put in a column. **A question written in a box shaped for an answer is a form that has been turned into a form, and a form that has been turned into a form is a lie with a seal on it** — and this is why the second honest useless form carries **no names** where the first carried eleven.

18. **The interface has now made twenty-three offers across nine batches and has never once registered a refusal of one**, and the count in Renner Sallow's book at Aurel is **thirteen** and the count of refusals in the second book he keeps at home is **twenty-two**, **and the twenty-third field is in neither book because no character answered it, and a girl of seventeen counted it and could not tell anybody which book to write it in.** Two messages in ten chapters, in Chapters 73 and 78. **The twenty-second is the twenty-first with the condition removed: a permanent exemption is a standing, and a body with a standing is a body that may be written to, and the refusal was in under a second and was the fourth interesting refusal in eighty days and is not the interesting half.** The interesting halves are the *repetition* — a form that repeats itself is establishing that it may — and the *silence*. *(Amends §14.10, §15.10 and §16.18: the silence count became **seven for seven**, and a review survived in daylight in front of about two hundred and forty people joined the list. Further amended by §18.18: the count is now **eight for eight**. The wall spoke twice in ten days and both times it wanted an office and both times it wanted a description.)*

19. **The number in the last column of a Crown table is the only seat in that table, and it can be answered with a question and not with a name.** Chapter 0075. The column asks for the name of one person who may be asked; **this district answered with the fifth item on a form of four items and a price, which is the only one of the five that anybody can use, and a clerk of that registry said in about nine seconds: that is not a person.** The answer is entered, sealed, and going to a building nine hundred miles away, and the notary entered that she decided to send it and that the decision took her a day and a half and that on the first day she could not have told you why.

20. **Adrian Vale is Stage 1 at the end of Volume 02 Batch 0003 and has gained nothing.** Eighty days in this world, no working performed, no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege, no seat, party to a trial licence that is **thirty days old** and gives him nothing, and **he has owned the failure of his own bar in a room, in daylight, with a date on the notice in his own hand, and has refused the excuse offered him in four seconds and asked for the refusal to be entered.** **The black line in his palm is seventy-nine days old and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch; the measurement column has twenty-four readings, both of the last two eight and seven-eighths, two steps in an unnamed mason's own hand before the number was read on the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth day of the new month, and one ruled line left, and the twenty-fifth reading has not been taken.**


## 18. Rules established in Volume 02, Chapters 81–90

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the ten days to the first of March. **They are binding from Chapter 91 onward.**

1. **A case is a case a carrier is paid for, and a case with nothing in it is a case nobody can charge to carry, and the emptiest thing in a case is the safest thing in it.** Chapter 0081. **The roll of strangers went with a hole in it and the hole is entered as a place where a number used to be, and no later writer may present that roll as complete or fill the hole.**

2. **A person is not owed the choosing of the room she says a thing in, and nobody is thanked for it.** Chapter 0081. **A name on a page is a thing that can be found, and a woman who says a name in a room says the second half of that cost herself, before anybody can, and the cost is not a fault and does not stop her.**

3. **The three refusals in the asking of a holder of a workway were put in an order on purpose, refusals first and the question last, and the answer and the refusals are on the same line because they are the same decision.** Chapter 0082. **A woman may describe her own work once, in her own words, in daylight, in a room; the description is hers; it may be taken back on a date; and nobody may re-ask her.** **The line that stopped the asking stands and no instrument may be read as removing it.**

4. **A year of certainty is a year of promises, and a level ditch is the promise, and a field is not a thing a person may be saved or written about by a man with no standing in it.** Chapter 0083. **Nobody in ninety days had asked the woman who works it whether she wants it, and she does not know, and she said a number out loud in daylight on the first of March anyway, and that is not a resolution.**

5. **THE UNDERTAKING OF THE LANTERN CONCORD, seven clauses and a date, is the false oath and it is on the page in full in `state/continuity.md`.** It makes a body of about a hundred and forty people permanent holder of the licence in exchange for taking the whole standing charge off a private debtor, at no cost to anybody. **Every clause in it is a sentence somebody in this city said out loud in the preceding eighty-four days, and it is neither refused nor accepted, and it is live, and it is voided in Chapter 0092 — which is inside Batch 0005, Chapters 91–100 — on the ground that its own condition was not met.** A later writer may not convict its writer, may not have anybody refuse it, may not turn it into a Crown instrument, and **may not collapse it into any of the five Crown instruments or into the Quiet Choir's design.** It is a seventh shape and it belongs to this city.

6. **A sentence somebody has heard is not a promise to that person any more, and a page made out of sentences a city has already said to itself hands the whole of them to somebody who did not hear them said.** Chapter 0084. **This is the batch's argument, and the man who wrote it first tried to call the document a false oath and was told he did not know the difference and did not use the word.**

7. **A guild that does not go home does not get old, does not want a different winter, cannot be refused by the people it costs, and cannot be asked separately about anything.** Chapters 0084, 0086, 0087. **That is a lock that a guild can close**, and it is Renna Ord's nine words, said in a yard on the tenth day of the new month, and it is Jonah Pike's sixth sentence arrived at from the other end twenty-eight days later without knowing. **A guild that can be asked has a standing, and a standing cannot be refused** — twenty-four words of Ilda Marsh, seventy, of Nine Elms.

8. **An undertaking is not in force until a person stands at the top of it and answers for it alone, and a document with no person at the top of it is either the safety or the hole, and the man who found it on the authority of his own signature history is not going to choose.** Chapter 0087. **This is the ground on which the false oath is void in Chapter 0091 and a later writer may not replace it with a better one.** It was found on a stall-keeper's authority and not out of a book, and that is entered.

9. **A document that costs nobody anything has no people in it, does not have to say what they are called, and has therefore already answered the eleventh question.** Chapter 0086, and it is the batch's central finding and it belongs to a girl of seventeen on no roll. **A document nobody can be angry at is a document nobody can check, and the two reasons are the same reason, and being unable to check and being unable to refuse are usually the same thing and came apart in this one.** Chapter 0089, in nine sentences of a woman who keeps a stall and whose name is not on anything.

10. **A page on a nail beside a form is two instruments in one arch, and an arch is a place, and a place is a room.** Chapter 0086. **A document is the four lines for the nine people at the front of the yard** — twenty-eight words of a girl of seventeen from the back, and it is the reason this city is going to have trouble in about four months. Standing test for any page in this series: **ask who can be angry at it, and if the answer is a body, ask again.**

11. **A correct document can be the problem, and a man who holds no office, no seat, no workway, no mark, no land right and no class privilege has nothing to answer a document with, and that is not the same as being relieved of one.** Chapter 0088. **There is nothing here to resign from and a man with nothing to resign is not a man being let go** — now spoken by the man it is about, about a document, in about two hundred and forty people. **Any later chapter that wants Adrian Vale to be relieved of something must first give him something, and a proposal on a slate is not a thing.**

12. **A document with no person in it can be argued about only by people who have time.** Entered as what a person said and not as a finding, by a notary who declined to enter it twice, **because a notary who enters her own findings is a notary who has become an office, and the last person in this city who became an office is on a nail in this yard with seven clauses and a date on it.**

13. **A form about a person may not be used on a document that has no person in it, and a form whose subject nobody can name is entered as an unfinished thing, and unfinished is a permitted state in this city.** Chapter 0089. **Four lines, drafted in about eleven minutes, and the notary's question was *who is it about*, and about four people in the room tried and nobody could answer.**

14. **A page that asks and names at the same time is a page a person can be angry at, and that is the first time in ninety days this city has written one.** Chapter 0090. **It is a list of what is owed, owed to named people and not to a body, dated, with a price written under it and not as another item, and it is inside a stone on a boundary that a household keeps, and a household may take it down on a date and is not asked why twice.** **Naming twelve people is a column for where twelve people are, and the protection is nine inches of stone and the fact that the person holding it is nobody's servant, and that is the price and it was entered as a price.**

15. **The cost column is a sentence with a number missing out of the middle of it, and a person is what has been standing behind it since the third day of the flood, and naming the person changes nothing and is the only honest entry available.** Chapter 0085, in five words in his own hand. **The column is entered for the eleventh time in one month, there is no twelfth entry, and a cost that stops is a cost that has stopped and is never to be entered as paid by anybody, on any day, for any reason.**

16. **You stand under a gate because somebody owes you and you have not been paid, and that is the oldest arithmetic in this city and the only reason a crew turns up, and forty men went under the near gate unpaid on the first of March and turned up.** Chapter 0085, on the page for the first time at a docker's own asking.

17. **The date on the nineteen shapes is the nineteenth day of the month after this one and it is the only thing on the slate.** Chapter 0090. **All nineteen are in four slates that four different people can read out loud, eleven are the same and eight are different, one of the eight is a name in a Hearthguard's hand about a hundred and forty years old, and the other seven have never been looked at.** *A later writer may not restage the reading of the nineteen shapes; see the flag in `state/batch-summaries/volume-02-batch-0004.md` §9.*

18. **The interface has now made twenty-five offers across ten batches and has never once registered a refusal of one**, and the count in Renner Sallow's book at Aurel is **thirteen** and the count of refusals in the second book he keeps at home is **twenty-two**, **and it did not go up in this batch, because both of this batch's fields were answered by nobody at all and a field that nobody answers is in neither book, and a girl of seventeen has now been unable to tell anybody which book a field goes in four times in ninety days.** Two messages in ten chapters, in Chapters 86 and 89. **Both of them were offers of a document, and both put nothing in the grammatical position where a cost goes.** The twenty-fourth is the undertaking on the nail in plain language, offered in the room in which the seven-clause version was being read. The twenty-fifth is a licence to draw a form about a person without telling the person, offered in the room that was drafting exactly that form about nobody. **There was nobody in either room who could refuse it, and the interface has now offered something unanswered twice running, which is the first time the same silence has happened twice.** *(Amends §14.10, §15.10, §16.18 and §17.18: the silence count is now **eight for eight**, and a city reading a document it cannot refuse joins the list. The wall spoke twice in ten days and both times it wanted a document and both times it put nothing where a cost goes.)* **The two silences are the batch's real argument: the mechanism the wall offers is the one the room is already holding, and the room's attention is the only place it can be refused, and the room did not look up.**

19. **The name *Single Witness* and the name *First Grammar* are spoken for the first time in prose in Chapter 0091, in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, and not before.** Chapters 81–90 use all three descriptions about a document and none of them names it, and **a later writer may not name it, may not collapse the three, and may not let a character guess the name aloud before Chapter 0091.**

20. **Adrian Vale is Stage 1 at the end of Volume 02 Batch 0004 and has gained nothing.** Ninety days in this world, no working performed, no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege, no seat, party to a trial licence that is **forty days old** and gives him nothing. **He proposed, in nineteen words, to put a person at the top of a document and make that person able to say no out loud in a room; a man of forty-two said on the page that the proposal on the table is the old form of the oath with a person in it, and that the man who proposed it will do it; and Adrian Vale has not answered that and has not been asked.** **The black line in his palm is eighty-nine days old and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch; the measurement column has twenty-four readings, both of the last two eight and seven-eighths, two steps in an unnamed mason's own hand before the number was read on the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth day of the new month, one ruled line left, and the twenty-fifth reading has not been taken and was not taken on the first of March.**

## 19. Rules established in Volume 02, Chapters 91–100

These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the ten days that close Volume 02. **They are binding from Chapter 101 onward and none of them replaces the First Grammar, the Quiet Choir, or Ivenn Marrow as the final human antagonist.** The volume's inheritance is `state/volume-02-close.md`.

1. **A page in this world can be aimed at a condition and at nothing else.** Chapter 0091. A form names a duty, a slate names a question, a page in a stone names a debt, nineteen words name a proposal, and none of the four can end a document, **because all four are about a person and a document with no person in it cannot be answered by a page about a person.** The standing test for any new instrument in this series: **ask what the page is about. If the answer is a person, it is a description.**

2. **THE VOID: a single sealed page that ends one document, once.** Chapter 0091 draws it, Chapter 0092 spends it, and it is Adrian Vale's Stage 2. It can be used **once and only once**, and **only on a thing that claims to be the work of a person**, and **its own condition is the ground and no other ground is used.** Nine witnesses, one of them named by herself and hers to take back. **It is spent, there is no second copy, the inside was folded twice, and nobody read it out.**

3. **The finding is entered as a record and not a finding and nobody may write why it worked.** The page does not say; the mechanism is not on any page in this world; and **a page that cannot say why it worked is the most dangerous page in this book and it stays that way.** No later chapter may have Adrian explain the mechanism, may not have a wall say anything about it, and may not have a character name it.

4. **The ground of a Void is a condition the document states about itself.** The undertaking of the Lantern Concord said in its own first sentence that it was a body and not a person, and an undertaking is not in force until a person stands at the top of it and answers for it alone. **A thing that argues for itself is a thing you can check. A thing that is silent can only be believed.** A later chapter may not invent a different ground, may not convict the man of sixty-one, may not have anybody refuse the document, and may not collapse the undertaking into any of the six Crown-side shapes or into the Quiet Choir's design.

5. **A Void exists in one place only, which is a notary's record, and there is no mark on the paper.** Chapter 0092: the undertaking was on the nail, unmarked, with the string untouched, and the voidness was in a book. **The record is worth more in this city than the artefact, because an artefact can be read by anybody and a notary can be asked.**

6. **ADRIAN VALE IS STAGE 2, PASSAGE HOLDER, IN THE DOING AND NOT IN A CEREMONY.** He can open a single-holder threshold: **one named living person per opening, one return mark carried, willing local witnesses who do not live with the person, a receiving community that has agreed, a fresh inspection, and the same route may not be opened twice in a row by him.** **No threshold was opened in Volume 02 and a later chapter may not open one casually.** He remains barred from the trial licence's own rosters and from the instrument of the twenty-second day of the new month, holds no land right, no title, no workway, no seat and no mark of his own, and is still the man who reads the number off a wall four feet back from it. **He does not reach Stage 3 in this volume and never will in it.** There is no office in Saltwake for what he now is and there has not been one since the fifty-third day of the new month.

7. **The cost of the stage is a person, and the batch says so on the page, and the person is Adrian Vale.** What he lost is **the knowing and not a road**: for ninety-three days he carried the idea that if he ever wanted to go back there was a way to work it out, and it is gone. **He has no return mark; the route home goes through a room with no door; the crossing is not written in this volume and the other world is not named in it; and the easy route home is spent, once, and is not offered again in this series.** This is **not** a second surrender of a personal return mark — he had none, and the ending plan's one personal-mark sacrifice is the Stage 5 event and is not this.

8. **A working leaves a residue in the person who did it, and at Stage 2 the residue is a perception he cannot use and cannot stop.** He sees a third line in the lower gallery that goes eleven inches from the dressed edge and stops, and can see where it would go, and may not use it. **This is the cost rendered as a fact of the world and not as a feeling, and a later chapter may not have it become a power.**

9. **A person at the top of a page cannot be asked whether they will have it, and that is the design flaw, and no chapter may rescue him from it.** The only instrument in this world that could have asked Adrian Vale whether he would stand at the top of the Void is the Void itself, and the Void does not ask, and nobody can make it ask, **and that is not a fault in the page; the page is doing exactly what it says.** This is the twentieth field's rule — a holder's work may be described without anybody asking her first — turned on the man who built the bar. **Any later instrument in this series that puts a person at the top of a page inherits the same hole, and a writer who wants a working performed by a named holder must find a different instrument and say how the asking happens.**

10. **Mina Okoye's sentence is used a second time in this book, by her, about somebody who is not her, and he asked her whether she meant it and nobody else was permitted to ask on his behalf.** *A hold is a working without a witness, and the residue of one goes into the person who did it.* **It may not be used a third time without a new asking of the same kind, and it may never be put in the mouth of another character, and no character may claim to have coined it.**

11. **The name *Single Witness* and the name *First Grammar* are on the page from Chapter 0091 and may be used in prose from Chapter 101 onward.** Both were said by Ivenn Marrow, in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, in his own hand, and both are in the book. **He is not convicted of anything, is not going to be, and is not made useless in any scene, and he asked for one thing to be entered and it was entered: that he cannot tell anybody afterwards whether he said them to help this city or to file it.** A later chapter may not have him explain either name, may not have him answer for them, and may not let a character guess either aloud.

12. **A name is not a refusal.** Ilda Marsh, seventy, of Nine Elms, in her own words, said twice in a hundred days: **a name is a description; a description is the column at the end of the page; the column is for where the person is; a column is a roll; a roll goes to Aurel in a wooden case four times a year.** **This city has spent a hundred days refusing to be described and has described the shape.** Any later use of either name must carry the same weight and must not become shorthand for a villain.

13. **A refusal with no date on it cannot be asked about, and a refusal with a date on it is an answer, and an answer is a description, and a description is a removal with a column for where the person is.** Chapter 0099, and it is the volume's last reversal: **the four words that kept seventeen people out of a column for twenty-four days were a refusal, and the review of the licence put them on a page with a heading and a date, and there is now a column for where the seventeen are with a space under the date for an officer's hand nine hundred miles away. Nobody undid it and nobody could.** This is the general form of the twentieth field's rule and it is the standing test for every dated protection in this series: **a protection with a date on it is a description with a date on it.**

14. **A holder may refuse the asking, once, without a reason, on a day that is on the page before the day; the asking is not repeated; the person is not on any roll by having been asked; and nobody may ask her about it again.** Chapter 0094, in Perry Lask's own words, said before anybody asked her anything, and **she would not let anybody make it a form.** The amendment is **worse for the instrument and better for the person, and both of those are true at once, and a page in this world is allowed to be both.**

15. **A notary of fifty drafted the procedure and drafted the amendment and may not grade her own paper, and she said that out loud.** The standing rule for this series: **the person who drafts an instrument is not the person who decides whether it is any good, and in Saltwake that person is Sera Vail, and the arrangement is now on the page and cannot be quietly dropped.**

16. **A seat with a name on it is a place a person can be angry at, and a person can only be angry at a seat if the room can reach them, and there is no road in this world that reaches Adrian Vale.** Fifth offer, fifth refusal, the instrument says nobody and has said nobody five times, **and he said eleven words and said nothing else about himself for the rest of the volume.** A later chapter may not restore the seat of holder of the pull without a room, in daylight, with a date on it, and may not have him ask for it.

17. **A refusal exercised in public and honoured is the first time in ninety-one days that this city watched a rule be obeyed against a person standing in front of them, and a person who is refused a thing may come back and read the refusal out in their own words, and that is not a complaint.** Chapter 0096. The protection is the reason being on a page, and **a reason on a page that is about a household and not about the person is the first description in a hundred days that is not a removal.**

18. **Two Wayhouses and one Open Hand exchange stand under local law, and none of them is Adrian Vale's, and all of them can be refused by the people who keep them, and none of them answers for another.** No share, no interest, and **a right of refusal that cannot be bought out, which cannot be administered and which is still the only one of the three worth having** — the reason it holds is that **it is the first rule anybody in this city has brought that nobody is going to check, and a rule nobody checks is a rule that everybody can get out from under.** These are the volume's durable gains and they are the pattern a Volume 03 institution has to match or beat.

19. **A description of a district that describes itself honestly is a district that will be described correctly, and the cheapest instrument in this city is a form that carries nothing anybody can act on.** Chapter 0098. **The emptiest document in a case is the safest one** and the rule has not been repealed since the first of March, **and a hole in a roll is a place where a number used to be and may not be filled.** The eighth column of a quarterly description may be answered with a question and not with a name, **a clerk may not ask a question and may not refuse one, and a person named in a question is not told the question exists.**

20. **A fee is a thing that tells you what an office thinks you are, and a form that is honest about what it does not know has nothing to put in the date column.** The fee of eleven ounces of silver is entered as owing with no date for the forty-first time and now has a reason, and the reason is new in a hundred days, and it is about a form and not about money.

21. **The interface has now made twenty-seven offers across eleven batches, and for the first time in eleven batches it has registered a refusal.** The count of refusals in the second book Renner Sallow keeps at home, never shown to anybody, **is twenty-three**; the count in his book at Aurel is **thirteen**; the difference of ten is entered and he has never been able to make it smaller. **The count of offers is not the count of refusals, a field that nobody answers goes in neither book, and a field answered by a person goes in one of two books and which one is not known in this city.** *(Amends §14.10, §15.10, §16.18, §17.18 and §18.18: the silence count is now **eleven for eleven**, and the wall spoke twice in this volume and both times it wanted a document and both times it put nothing where a cost goes, and the second of the two was the first thing it has ever said that anybody in this city refused.)*

22. **The measurement column is unchanged and still not continuous.** Twenty-four readings, both of the last two eight and seven-eighths, two steps in an unnamed mason's hand on the eighteenth and the twenty-fourth day of the new month, one ruled line left. **The twenty-fifth reading has not been taken on the first of March, on the ninth day of the month after this one, or on any day in Volume 02, and the four who read the join-line disagreed for the fourth time since the first of March and all four entries stand.** A dressed join does not narrow; it gets dressed. It has never spoken.

23. **The open dates at the end of Volume 02, all binding:** **the fourth day of the month after this one** is the workway review date and has been used; **the nineteenth day of the month after this one** is the date on the nineteen shapes and about nine people go up a nine-mile road on it in a month that is not March; **the sixteenth day of the month after this one** is on the page nailed inside the first Wayhouse; **the twenty-second day of the month after this one** is in chalk on a village door at Nine Elms and is attached to a document that is void; and the **Registry hearing is about eight weeks from the hundredth day of the flood** and there is one hearing and one clock.

24. **Adrian Vale at the end of Volume 02 is Stage 2 with nothing and no road.** One working performed, in a converted salt store, in daylight, in front of about two hundred and forty people. No mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege, no seat, no office, no roster, and **no road**; on no roster of the licence and on no roster of the instrument of the twenty-second day of the new month, at his own request, in his own hand, with the date on it. **The black line in his palm is ninety-nine days old and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch.** The measurement column stands. The door has been opened twice in a hundred days and the hundredth day was not one of them.

## 20. Rules established in Volume 03, Chapters 101–110

**These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the first ten days of Volume 03. None of them replaces the First Grammar, the Quiet Choir, or Ivenn Marrow as the final human antagonist, and none of them is a new power for anybody.** They are binding from Chapter 111 onward. The batch's own record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-03-batch-0001.md`.

1. **THE VOID STAYS SPENT AND THE FAILURE TO EXPLAIN IT IS THE VOLUME'S ENGINE.** Five rooms in this city have now tried to work out how a page ended a document, on the twelfth day of the month after this one, and all five failed in the same place: **the page is a heading and seven lines and it does not say what it does.** The count of rooms is entered with the date on it because a city that has failed at a thing four times and has never written the four down will fail a fifth time in the same place. **No chapter in this series may show the page working, may have anybody reproduce it, and may not have anybody produce a mechanism. A chapter that explains it has replaced the most dangerous page in the book with a diagram, and that is a fault and not a reveal.** A resemblance is not a mechanism, and the finding of the thirteenth day of the month after this one is that **the reason nobody is going to find out is that the finding out is the using.**

2. **A page in this world can only be aimed at a condition, and a condition is the only thing a page here can be pointed at that is not a person — and a person cannot be found by a condition.** Chapter 0104, ten days of four people trying to write down the room at the far end of the Narrow Mark. The description they produced is **four lines and every line of it is about the eleven people who have been in the room and not about the room**, and that is entered as the finding: **the four lines are the shape of the room after eleven people have been through it.** The standing test is unchanged and is now applied to a room as well as to a document: **ask what the page is about. If the answer is a person, it is a description. If the answer is the people who have looked at it, it is also a description.**

3. **A gate and a page are the same kind of thing and are not the same kind of thing, and the comparison was refused in about four seconds by the man who has done it.** A gate takes a person standing behind it and does not ask; a page ended a document and did not ask the man standing at the top of it. **That is a resemblance.** Adrian Vale entered the refusal in a column four lines high and the entry stands.

4. **A question that asks for names is unanswerable in a district that has spent a hundred and six days not writing names down, and the not-writing was the protection.** Chapter 0106, and it is the volume's hinge. Two ways exist to refuse the eighth column's question — put a refusal on a dated page in a room, or put the eight names on a page so the office can stop asking — **and both of them were refused on purpose, with the date on the entry, and the reason is not that the names are lost.** Seven of the eight are nine miles up a road in a wall and one of them is a name in a Hearthguard's grandfather's hand. **A refusal with a date on it is an answer, and an answer is a description, and that is the whole of the difficulty and it is now on the page in the man who asked for the question's own words.**

5. **A person is the only shape that can be named, and therefore the only shape that can be found, and therefore the moment somebody in a city agrees to carry a cost the city has written down where that cost is.** Chapter 0105, a notary of fifty, and the finding of the batch: **every institution in this city is a person and every one of them is tired.** Neither half of that is a complaint. **A person may carry a cost; there is no other instrument. And if nobody in a city is allowed to carry anything, then in four months there are four hundred people in a city where a cost exists and no person holds it, and a cost that nobody holds is not a cost that has stopped — it is a cost that has got nobody.** Both errors are refused in the same room on the same afternoon.

6. **THE ANSWER TO MOST OF WHAT THIS CITY CANNOT SETTLE IS NOT A PERSON, AND THEREFORE CANNOT BE ASKED ANYTHING.** Chapter 0107, fifteen words, and the whole of a hundred and seven days: **a number cannot be asked whether it will have it; a page cannot be asked whether it will have it; a person can.** A number and a page are alike in that neither can be asked. **A number is safe and a page is not, and the difference is that a number cannot end anything and a page can, and there is one of the second kind in this city and it is spent.** The difference of ten between the two books a clerk of the Registry keeps is entered as **the safest thing in this city** and he has entered that he can change his half of it alone, tonight, in a room upstairs, and that he is not going to, **and that the difference was nine before the first registered refusal and is ten now and has never been made smaller.**

7. **Both names are in ordinary use from Chapter 0101 and are not explained.** *Single Witness* is the patch; *First Grammar* is what carries it. **A name that has been given may be repeated by a person who was in the room when it was given, in the local language, and may not be guessed aloud by anybody who was not.** A woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by the wharf was asked the two words on a wharf in the rain and refused to say them, and said why in about four seconds. Ivenn Marrow has still not explained either name and has now said on the page that he is not going to, and has asked for that to be entered a second time in front of nine people. **He is not convicted of anything and is not going to be.**

8. **There is no column in the world for an answer, and this city has been protected by that for a hundred and forty years, and the protection is that nothing comes back.** Chapter 0101, on a wharf. A case goes down a river four times a year and the only thing that has ever come back up one is a count. **The reason nobody can be found is the same fact as the reason nothing can be audited, and the second one is the first one with a river in the middle of it.** A quarter is about thirteen weeks and the Registry hearing is about eight, **so the next time this city can put anything at all in front of that office is about five weeks after the room in which it would have mattered sits down and goes home.**

9. **A clerk of that office may not ask a question and may not refuse one, and a person named in a question is not told the question exists, and no person is named in this one.** Unchanged and binding. **The eighth column is on a river and it may not be intercepted, answered, translated into the local language in a room where about two hundred and forty people can hear it, or taken back.** A batch may pay the cost of getting it there and may not touch it.

10. **THE FOUR WHO READ THE JOIN-LINE ARE STILL FOUR AND ONLY FOUR, AND THEY HAVE NOW DISAGREED FIVE TIMES SINCE THE FIRST OF MARCH, AND ALL FIVE ENTRIES STAND.** Chapters 0103 and 0110. Three at a line and Peth Lomas a sixteenth under, five times, and he says he was under the first time and under the fifth time and that nothing in between moved him. **The twenty-fifth reading has not been taken and one ruled line is still ruled and still empty. A dressed join does not narrow; it gets dressed.** A later chapter may not add a fifth reader and may not take the twenty-fifth reading without a reason written on the page in that scene.

11. **THE INTERFACE HAS MADE TWENTY-NINE OFFERS AND HAS REGISTERED ONE REFUSAL, AND BOTH OF ITS NEW OFFERS IN THIS BATCH WERE ACCEPTED BY A PERSON IN FOUR SECONDS AND NEITHER ACCEPTANCE MOVED ANYTHING.** The twenty-eighth was accepted by a bone-setter and the twenty-ninth by a clerk of the Registry, and **the count of refusals in the second book kept at home is still twenty-three.** The twenty-eighth: *a cost that has no figure on the end of it may be carried by a person who was not there when it was entered. The person is not asked. The person is not described by it. The person is not on any roll. The cost is the carrying. The cost is entered. There is no further cost.* The twenty-ninth: *a count may be corrected by the person who entered it. The person is not asked to agree with anybody. The person is not described by the count. The cost is that the correction is the person's alone and nobody has to sign it. The cost is entered. There is no further cost.* **Both put nothing where a cost goes and both are true, and a field answered by a person goes in one of two books and nobody in this city knows which, and it is entered that a clerk has now been unable to say which one six times in a hundred and five days.** *(Amends §14.10, §15.10, §16.18, §17.18, §18.18 and §19.21: the silence count is now **nineteen for nineteen**, counted silences and not days; the wall spoke twice in this batch and a day the wall speaks on is not a silence however hard the day was.)*

12. **A DATE ON A PAGE IS A DOOR AND NOT A PROMISE, AND A DOOR THAT DOES NOT OPEN ON THE DAY IS STILL A PAGE AND IS STILL DOING ITS WORK.** Chapter 0110. The date on the page nailed inside the first Wayhouse was the sixteenth day of the month after this one. **It came and went, the household did not close the house, nobody was told why, and nobody asked, and the page is still on the wall of that house.** There is no instrument in four hundred miles that can make a household keep a door open either, and this city built that on the same afternoon. **The two institutions in this city that will still be here in four months are the one where nobody asked and the one nobody can carry.**

13. **THE TWELVE NAMES ON THE SECOND PAGE IN THE STONE ARE TWELVE PEOPLE AND NOT TWELVE DEBTS, AND THE PAGE IS THE ONLY ROLL IN THIS CITY THAT DOES NOT GO TO AUREL.** Chapter 0106. Twelve lines, twelve people, one of whom has two debts on one line. **It is a description with a date on it and every name on it is a place where a person is; it is nailed inside a stone at a fork nine miles up a road that eleven households keep and that is on no page; and the reason the copy in a girl's book is headed *it is a copy and the stone does not travel* is that a book with twelve names in it is a door into a list, and the twelve would be looked at and the eight would be looked for.** The count of seven cuts went onto that page on the twentieth day of the month after this one, under the twelve, under the price of salt, in six lines, with a line under it saying that it is a count and not a debt and the word *nobody* on the line above it.

14. **A FINDING ABOUT A FAILURE IS THE ONLY KIND OF FINDING THIS CITY HAS EVER WRITTEN DOWN.** Chapter 0110, a mason of thirty-four years: **nobody can say what the seven cuts mean, and a thing this city cannot answer has to be on a page or it does not exist, and the finding is that nobody can answer it.** Six lines of count went on a page in a stone and not one line of meaning, and nobody wrote anything on the wall, and **a wall is the cheapest thing in this city and there is already one.**

15. **ADRIAN VALE IS STILL STAGE 2 AND STILL HAS NO ROAD, AND HE PERFORMED NO WORKING IN ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS.** He is barred from every roster, holds no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege and no seat, and there is no office in Saltwake for what he is. **He may not open a threshold in this volume casually, may not be rescued from the fact that a person at the top of a page cannot be asked whether they will have it, and the crossing and the other world are not written in these ten days and must not be.** The third line in the lower gallery still goes eleven inches from the dressed edge and stops and may not be used and he is not going to be able to stop seeing it. **The black line in his palm is one hundred days old at Chapter 0101 and one hundred and nine at the end of Chapter 0110 and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch at any point.** His best sentence in the batch is a refusal in four seconds on a step, and the whole of what he did on a nine-mile road was ask a docker to read him a date off a slate.

## 21. Rules established in Volume 03, Chapters 111–120

**These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the second ten days of Volume 03. None of them replaces the First Grammar, the Quiet Choir, or Ivenn Marrow as the final human antagonist, none of them is a new power for anybody, and none of them is a mechanism for the Void.** They are binding from Chapter 121 onward. The batch's own record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-03-batch-0002.md`.

1. **A CASE CAME UP A RIVER, AND IT CANNOT BE CHECKED AND IT CANNOT BE OPENED, AND THAT IS THE POINT OF IT.** Chapter 0116, the twenty-sixth day of the month after this one, the hundred and sixteenth day of the flood. **A case goes down four times a year and has never come up in a hundred and forty years, and the protection this city has relied on — that nothing comes back — has arrived on its own account.** It crossed in the middle of the water with the case that went down on the eighth day of the month after this one and neither of the two overtook the other, and four people on a quay gave the account separately and agree, and a notary entered that four agreeing accounts are not a record. **A case that is opened early is a case that has been read**, and there is no procedure in four hundred miles for opening a thing an office has sent, so the box is not opened and may not be opened before the hearing.
2. **THE EIGHTH SHAPE, DATED: the Registry's direction of the nineteenth day of the new month, which was the forty-fourth day of the flood, seventy-two days on the water.** It says the district shall name one person to be heard at the hearing, and that where no person is named the district shall be described by a number. **It is not the notice of the twenty-eighth day of this flood, and it is not the fifth thing that office put in a case on the fifty-second day of the new month, and it does not collapse into any of the seven.** A ninth shape is dated beside it: **a boundary page of the village of Nine Elms, about a hundred and forty years old, a local record and not a Crown instrument**, headed with the day of the public act with witnesses that is also cut into a stone at a fork at Four Elms.
3. **A TRANSLATION IS A DESCRIPTION. A DESCRIPTION IS A REMOVAL WITH A COLUMN FOR WHERE THE PERSON IS. THE COLUMN IS ADRIAN VALE.** Chapter 0119, which is the volume's midpoint as `outline/series.md` states it, and **it was paid by his own competence and not by a fault in him.** A boundary form has one place for a count and one place for an assent and they are the same place, and the word in that place is the word for a count, so a village's agreement is a number of men having been taken. **A form in this world is not a sentence and cannot be read as one; a form is a set of places, and the only person in that room who can tell a form from a sentence is a man from another world who has spent a hundred and nineteen days learning the difference, two lines below a man of forty-one in a very good coat who has read every book that office keeps and who said out loud that he could not read it and would not guess at it in a room. **He is not the only person in four hundred miles and does not say so, and the chapter treats the rarity as the danger and not as a comfort.** He read it out in about nine minutes and nobody improved on it, and it is entered as what a person said and not as a finding, and **he said in four minutes that he is not going to be able to stop.**
4. **A HEARING IS A ROOM, AND A ROOM IS THE ONLY INSTRUMENT IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES THAT AN OFFICE NINE HUNDRED MILES AWAY CANNOT REWRITE.** Chapter 0111, four seconds, and the reason is that a room is not a document and a document is not a place. **The clock runs the wrong way round: a hearing is in thirty-four days at the close of this batch, the next case goes out at the quarter, and a quarter is about thirteen weeks, so the next time this city can put anything at all in front of that office is about five weeks after the hearing.** Nothing can be sent and nothing can be prepared, because anything prepared has to go into a book in this city or a stone nine miles up a road.
5. **A LIST OF WHO WILL STAND IN A ROOM IS A REMOVAL WITH A COLUMN FOR WHERE THE PERSON IS, and the answer is a number of people and not a list.** Chapter 0115. About nine people got to a list inside nine minutes and crossed it out inside four seconds. **The number nine is entered with a date on it and with no person at the top of it**, which makes it the first page in a hundred and twenty days with a number on it and nobody standing above the number, and **the opposite of the most dangerous page in this city, and the same kind of page.** A number cannot be asked whether it will have it; a page cannot; a person can. **And a household is a person, and a household can be asked and a household can refuse, which is why nine people cannot be carried: every one of them is somebody's household in the way that matters.**
6. **A COST COLUMN WITH NO ROOM IN IT CAN DECIDE A SEASON, AND NOBODY CAN ENTER THAT THE MEN WHO WORKED IT ARE OWED ANYTHING.** Chapter 0120. **The rule drawn under the eleventh line settled in the season of ninety-one acres out of a hundred and forty for the second time in thirty-five days, by nobody, and the person the field is on was standing in the room and was asked nothing and said nothing and did not turn round.** The first time was on the first of March, which was the eighty-fifth day of the flood, at a gateway, in daylight, in the local language, by the person the field is on, and a number was said once and then not said again. **Both settlements are canon and neither may be presented as the only one. The difference between them is the finding: a person can be asked whether she will have it and a rule under a line cannot be asked anything, and the rule was ruled and empty on the first of March as well.** A rule cannot be refused, and it is not known in this city whether that is because the rules are right or because the rules are not people.
7. **A MAN WHO HAS STOPPED COMING IS NOT A COST THAT HAS STOPPED — thirteen words, a docker of fifty-eight who cannot read, and the room did not check the count.** Chapter 0114. **A man who is not paid and a man who will not come are two different men, and the rule that stops a cost being entered is the rule that makes an unpaid man look like a man who will not come.** Thirty-nine of forty men turn up; the foreman of the day crew, who has said four words under that gate every working day since the forty-sixth day of the new month, did not.
8. **A DATE ON A DOOR IS NEITHER A PAGE NOR A DOOR.** Chapter 0112, the twenty-second day of the month after this one. A page in this world can be taken down on a date by the household that keeps it and nobody is asked why twice, and a door cannot, and a chalk mark cannot be washed off and cannot be explained. **The two are the same sentence with the words moved about, and one of them is a protection and the other is a thing nobody can do anything with.** Nine households of Nine Elms refused a fourth time and **the fourth is a person and not a village**, and nobody in this city can say what a person's fourth refusal is.
9. **A CASE WITH A NAME IN IT CAN BE CARRIED — nine seconds, a carrier of the second table.** Chapter 0113. This is why the eighth column's question is unanswerable here and why the answer it gets back will be a correct number about a district with eight people not in it. **A cooperative with no share, no interest and a right of refusal that cannot be bought out is the only instrument in this city that can refuse to carry nine names**, and it refused in about four seconds and put the refusal on a board with no name on it.
10. **A DESCRIPTION OF AN OBJECT IS ABOUT THE PEOPLE WHO HAVE HANDLED IT, and this city has now done this twice.** Chapter 0117 restages the four lines about a room at the far end of a seam on a wooden box: six attempts, about four hours, four people, **a nineteen-line page of which sixteen lines are about nine people.** The count of attempts is six and is entered with the date on it. The standing test is unchanged and now covers objects as well as rooms and documents: **ask what the page is about. If the answer is a person, it is a description. If the answer is the people who have looked at it, or handled it, it is also a description.**
11. **THE VOID STAYS SPENT, AND A SIXTH ROOM WAS WANTED AND NOT HELD.** Five rooms have failed to explain a page that ended a document and all five failed in the same place, and **a sixth was wanted in the room over a market on the twenty-seventh day of the month after this one and was not held, because about four people could not agree on a question in nine minutes, which is how long the last four took.** **No chapter may show the page working, reproduce it, or produce a mechanism. The count of rooms on the page is five.** About four months of failing is on the clock and a failure that is entered with a date is the whole of what this city has instead of an explanation.
12. **THE INTERFACE HAS MADE THIRTY OFFERS ACROSS THIRTEEN BATCHS, HAS REGISTERED ONE REFUSAL, AND THE THIRTIETH PUT A COST WHERE A COST GOES FOR THE FIRST TIME IN THE BOOK.** The thirtieth, Chapter 0116: *A thing that has come up a river may be described by any person who has seen it. The person who describes it is not described by it. The person is not on any roll. The cost is that the description is the only account of the thing that will exist. The cost is entered. There is no further cost.* **Accepted in about four seconds by a notary of fifty and not refused**, and the count of refusals in the second book kept at home is still **twenty-three**, the count at Aurel is **thirteen**, and the difference is **ten** and was nine before the first registered refusal and has never been made smaller. **The cost was paid on the following day in about four hours by four people and the account that will exist is nineteen lines long and sixteen of them are about nine people.**
13. **TWENTY-EIGHT SILENCES IN A ROW, TWENTY-EIGHT FOR TWENTY-EIGHT**, counted silences and not days, and a day the wall speaks on is not a silence however hard the day was. The wall spoke once in these ten days, on the twenty-sixth day of the month after this one. *(Amends §14.10, §15.10, §16.18, §17.18, §18.18, §19.21 and §20.11: all eight must be changed together.)*
14. **ADRIAN VALE IS STILL STAGE 2 AND STILL HAS NO ROAD, AND HE PERFORMED NO WORKING IN ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS.** He holds no mark of his own, no workway, no title, no land right, no class privilege and no seat, and **he is now entered on the page as a column.** He may not open a threshold in this volume casually, may not be rescued from the fact that a person at the top of a page cannot be asked whether they will have it, **and may not be rescued from the fact that a translation is a description either: nobody may hand him an instrument that lets him stop reading, and the search for one would be a rescue and not a scene.** The crossing and the other world are not written in these ten days and must not be. **The black line in his palm is one hundred and ten days old at Chapter 0111 and one hundred and nineteen at the end of Chapter 0120 and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch at any point, and the line's age is day minus one and is not the length of his stay in this world, which is the same figure in this batch only by coincidence and must not be run into the other.**
15. **THE MEASUREMENT COLUMN, THE FOUR READERS, THE FIVE LOCK SITES, THE KNOCKS, AND THE SIX CROWN-SIDE SHAPES PLUS A SEVENTH ARE ALL UNCHANGED AND NONE OF THEM IS COLLAPSED IN THIS BATCH.** The four disagreed for the sixth time since the first of March and **the sixth was about the edge and not the number**, so Peth Lomas remains under five times out of six, all six entries stand, and the twenty-fifth reading was not taken on any of these ten days. **The Narrow Mark has been opened twice in a hundred and nineteen days and not a third time. The boards under the chapel have not been lifted since the fortieth day of this flood. The chapel stone said nothing and is not repaired. The knocks were not used, not reported and not mentioned.** The undertaking of the Lantern Concord is a seventh shape, is ours, is void and is not withdrawn, and **the undertaking was not sent to anybody and no office has been told that it is void.**

## 22. Rules established in Volume 03, Chapters 121–130

**These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the third ten days of Volume 03, which are the last ten days before the hearing. None of them replaces the First Grammar, the Quiet Choir, or Ivenn Marrow as the final human antagonist, none of them is a new power for anybody, and none of them is a mechanism for the Void.** They are binding from Chapter 131 onward. The batch's own record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-03-batch-0003.md`.

1. **STANDING NEAR A THING IS A PRACTICE AND A PRACTICE IS A TOLL.** Chapter 0121. A box that has been in a room with a door for five days and cannot be opened is a thing nobody is waiting for and a thing everybody is standing near, and **every person who stands near it adds a line to the only account of it that will exist**, and that account is a nineteen-line page of which three lines are about the box and sixteen are about nine people. **This is the thirtieth offer's cost arriving late and continuously and it does not need a new offer to keep arriving.**
2. **A CHAIR WITH NO DATE ON IT IS A PAGE NOBODY CAN BE ANGRY AT, AND A PAGE WITH A DATE ON IT CAN BE TAKEN DOWN ON A DATE, AND A CHAIR IS THE ONLY THING IN THIS CITY THAT IS ALLOWED TO BE NOTHING.** Chapter 0121, in four seconds, when a man of thirty-two asked for a date on a chair somebody had put on a landing. **Never give a new piece of this city's furniture a date unless the chapter is about what the date makes it possible to remove.**
3. **A DECISION NOT TO DO SOMETHING IS A PAGE, AND THIS CITY HAD NOT WRITTEN ONE ABOUT ITSELF BEFORE THE THIRTY-SECOND DAY OF THE MONTH AFTER THIS ONE.** Chapter 0122: a decision not to carry a word to a foreman who is owed thirty-seven days, on a slate, with a date on it and no name on it. **Three pages about this city now exist and all three are in books that cannot travel with dates on them and no names.**
4. **A PAGE IN A STONE CANNOT BE CORRECTED, AND THE ONLY CORRECTION AVAILABLE TO ANYBODY IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES IS TO ASK THE KEEPING HOUSEHOLD TO TAKE IT DOWN ON A DATE, AND NOBODY HAS EVER ASKED THAT HOUSEHOLD ANYTHING.** Chapter 0122. **NINE INCHES OF STONE IS NOT A SAFE PLACE FOR ANYTHING**, and the reason a household has never been asked is that asking is how you find out it can say no, and this city would rather have a page it can never check. *The archive of this city is nine inches of stone and a household nobody asked, and a later chapter may not pretend the stone is safe.*
5. **A NAME AT THE TOP OF A PAGE CAN BE ARGUED WITH, AND A PAGE A PERSON CAN BE ANGRY AT IS ALSO A PAGE THAT CAN BE USED AGAINST THE PERSON, AND THOSE ARE THE SAME PROTECTION.** Chapter 0123, the first self-naming in a hundred and twenty-three days, made by a person on no roll about a page that is about nine people, on a local page that cannot travel. **A page with nobody at the top of it is the safest page this city has built precisely because it cannot be corrected, and a name at the top of it is the oldest protection there is precisely because a person can be held to it.** Both halves are on the page now and only one of them was a hundred and twenty-three days ago.
6. **A HEARING IS A ROOM AND AN ASKING IS A KITCHEN, AND THE ONLY INSTRUMENT THIS CITY HAS IS A ROOM, AND A ROOM IS THE WRONG SHAPE FOR AN ASKING.** Chapter 0125. **Nine people hearing an answer is eight other people hearing an answer.** Nothing will be written down about any answer, by anybody, in any book, on any board, in any stone, or in any column. **And the cost of that is that if the number is eight on the day, nobody will ever be able to say who was not in the room.**
7. **THE TWO PEOPLE WHO CAN CARRY AN ANSWER ANYWHERE ARE THE TWO PEOPLE WHO MAY NOT ASK, AND BOTH REASONS ARE THE SAME REASON.** Chapter 0125. A person on no roster who asks nine households a question is a person who is asking, and asking is what a chain is made of; and a man standing at the junction of two systems of writing is the man who will say what nine people meant, and a man who says what nine people meant is a description of nine people. **Adrian Vale has entered, with the date on it, that he has not asked one person one question in a hundred and twenty-nine days.**
8. **A SENTENCE BECOMES A NAME WHEN TWO PEOPLE HAVE SAID IT IN ONE ROOM, and this city's protection against a boy's sentence becoming a name is that it has not put its rooms together.** Chapter 0126. **A city which puts its rooms together is a city with an index, and an index is a column.**
9. **AN ANSWER THAT EXISTS AND HAS NOWHERE TO GO IS THE SAME BEAT AS AN ANSWER THAT DOES NOT EXIST, AND THE BETTER ONE.** Chapter 0126. *That is the hearing*, four more words in the local language which nobody counted, then *we will stand there*. **It is on a boy's slate and in no book, it has never been improved on, it has not been repeated, and the man who said it has said in a room that he is not going to say it again, because a thing said twice in a room has an account.** A hearing is a room and a room is gone in a day; a slate goes in a book that travels; a stone nine miles up a road cannot be read; four ears is not a record and is not nothing either. **It is on the slate because there is nowhere else and not because it is safe.**
10. **A PERSON'S FOURTH REFUSAL IS A REFUSAL WITH NOTHING UNDER IT, AND IT IS THE STRONGEST REFUSAL THIS CITY HAS AND IT IS HELD UP BY LUCK.** Chapter 0128, said by a woman of seventy of a village of nine households, in a kitchen, in daylight, on a day she did not choose. **A refusal with a document under it can be un-refused by a person who reads the document; a refusal with nothing under it can be un-refused by anybody who never heard it.** The first three were refusals of a document and the no went into the same thing as the notice. **A document is the only thing that can be argued with and a person is the only thing that can be asked, and this is the first refusal in the book that has chosen the second without a page under it.**
11. **THE VOID STAYS SPENT, SIX ROOMS HAVE NOW FAILED IN THE SAME PLACE, AND THE SIXTH THING THEY TRIED IS *WHO WANTED IT TO WORK*, AND THE ANSWER IS THAT NOBODY IN THIS CITY EVER HAS.** Chapter 0127. A description of a thing is made by people who want the thing to be different from what it is, and **not one of the nine people in that room ever wanted the page to have not worked, which is why five rooms and then a sixth have got nowhere, and it is not because the page is hard.** The count of rooms on the page is **six**; a seventh was wanted and not held. **No chapter may show the page working, reproduce it, or produce a mechanism, and the one sentence that came closest must not be written down.** A room nobody can be angry at is the reason four hours were allowed and is the reason they produced nothing, and the same shape is in about nine pages in this city and in a board with no names and a chair on a landing and a stone nine miles up a road.
12. **THE INTERFACE HAS MADE THIRTY-ONE OFFERS, HAS NOW REGISTERED TWO REFUSALS OUT LOUD, AND THE SECOND OF THEM IS THE THIRTY-FIRST, WHICH WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS BY A PERSON WHO WAS NOT ADRIAN VALE, AND THE REFUSAL COST HER SOMETHING SHE NAMED HERSELF.** The first refusal out loud was the twenty-seventh, on the eighth day of the month after this one in Volume 02. **The count of twenty-four in the clerk's second book and the count of two refused out loud are two different numbers and a chapter may not confuse them: the twenty-four is a column in a book and the two is how many of the thirty-one things the wall has said in this city a person has said no to in a room.** Chapter 0127: *A thing that has failed in five rooms may be described by one person, once. The person who describes it is not described by it. The person is not on any roll. **The cost is that one person is then the account of it, and every later account of it will be checked against that one person, and that person will not be asked whether they would have it.** The cost is entered. There is no further cost.* **The count of refusals in the second book a clerk of the Registry keeps at home went from twenty-three to twenty-four, the count at Aurel is thirteen, the difference is eleven, it was ten for about a hundred days, it was nine before the first registered refusal, and it has never been made smaller.** The cost paid was that the person who refused has said out loud in a room that she knows what a page that ends a document is, and a person who knows that is a column. **Any later refusal out loud in this book must cost somebody something specific and the chapter must say what, and a refusal that costs nothing is not a refusal in this book's sense.**
13. **THIRTY-SEVEN SILENCES IN A ROW, THIRTY-SEVEN FOR THIRTY-SEVEN**, counted silences and not days, and a day the wall speaks on is not a silence however hard the day was. The wall spoke once in these ten days, on the thirty-seventh day of the month after this one, and a person refused it in about four seconds, and **that day does not move the count: it carries the same number as the day before it and the count stands where it stood.** The running count across the ten days is twenty-nine, thirty, thirty-one, thirty-two, thirty-three, thirty-four, **thirty-four**, thirty-five, thirty-six, thirty-seven, and the repeat is the rule and not an error, exactly as the repeat on the twenty-sixth day of Batch 0002 was. *(Amends §14.10, §15.10, §16.18, §17.18, §18.18, §19.21, §20.11 and §21.13: all eight must be changed together, and all eight now read thirty-eight for thirty-eight and are corrected here. The count was thirty-eight in this section and in seven others because Chapter 0127's ledger stated the rule and broke it in the same sentence; see `reviews/volume-03/batch-0003.md` finding 1.)*
14. **ADRIAN VALE IS STILL STAGE 2 AND STILL HAS NO ROAD, AND HE PERFORMED NO WORKING IN ANY OF THESE TEN DAYS, AND THE COST OF THE READING FELL ON PEOPLE WHO ARE NOT HIM FOUR TIMES.** Chapter 0123: **he was asked to read a page out loud in the local language and said no in about four seconds, because a page with a girl's name on it does not get his voice on it, and that is a refusal of an act and not a rescue, and she read her own page out badly instead.** He may not be rescued from the fact that a translation is a description, **nobody may hand him an instrument that lets him stop reading, and the search for one would be a rescue and not a scene.** **No chapter in this batch rescued him and no chapter produced such an instrument, and the four people the cost fell on are a woman about thirty-four at a wharf, a girl of seventeen, a woman of seventy at Nine Elms, and a woman who keeps a public register.** The crossing and the other world are not written in these ten days and must not be. **The black line in his palm is one hundred and twenty days old at Chapter 0121 and one hundred and twenty-nine at the end of Chapter 0130 and has not moved one sixteenth of an inch at any point, and the line's age is day minus one and is not the length of his stay in this world, which is the same figure in this batch only by coincidence and must not be run into the other.**
15. **THE MEASUREMENT COLUMN, THE FOUR READERS, THE FIVE LOCK SITES, THE KNOCKS, AND THE TEN SHAPES ARE ALL UNCHANGED AND NONE OF THEM IS COLLAPSED IN THIS BATCH.** The four readers disagreed six times since the first of March, **the sixth was about the edge and not the number**, so Peth Lomas remains under five times out of six, all six entries stand, and the twenty-fifth reading was not taken on any of these ten days and has not been taken for forty-five days. **The Narrow Mark has been opened twice in a hundred and thirty days and not a third time. The boards under the chapel have not been lifted since the fortieth day of this flood. The chapel stone said nothing and is not repaired. The knocks were not used, not reported, not mentioned, and the word does not appear once in Chapters 121–130.** The undertaking of the Lantern Concord is a seventh shape, is ours, is void and is not withdrawn, and no office has been told. **A TENTH SHAPE IS DATED IN THIS BATCH — a table in a single sheet folded into eight, carried privately in a coat for seven years, of every attempt this city has made to move the old river boundary, eleven lines, local, not a Crown instrument, not the eighth shape, not the ninth shape, not read out and not going into a case — and all ten are named and dated on the page in the entry that says they are new.**
16. **THE HEARING IS ON OR ABOUT DAY 154 AND IS TWENTY-FOUR DAYS FROM THE CLOSE OF THIS BATCH, AND NO CHAPTER IN THIS BATCH HELD IT, STAGED IT, REHEARSED IT, NAMED ITS OUTCOME OR RESOLVED ANYTHING THAT BELONGS TO IT.** Chapter 0130's ledger and `state/batch-summaries/volume-03-batch-0003.md` §1 and §13. **The next batch is the last batch of the volume and it has to carry the volume's climax: Adrian's refusal to become a Crown-appointed interpreter with unlimited authority, with the hearing recording the arrivals as named guests under a revocable compact.**

## 23. Rules established in Volume 03, Chapters 131–150

**These are additions to the sections above, not replacements, and they are on the page in the last twenty days of Volume 03, which close the volume. None of them replaces the First Grammar, the Quiet Choir, or Ivenn Marrow as the final human antagonist, none of them is a new power for anybody, and none of them is a mechanism for the Void.** They are binding from Chapter 0151 onward. The batch's own record, which is also the volume's batch record, is `state/batch-summaries/volume-03-batch-0004.md`.

1. **A HEARING IS A ROOM, IT WAS HELD, AND IT IS OVER. Day 154, the hundred and fifty-fourth day of the flood, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, about nine people in it, and about two hundred and forty people able to hear out of the hall, the landing and the market below it.** There was one hearing and one clock. **The next time this city can put anything at all in front of the office that held it is about five weeks from the hearing, which is the quarter, and that interval is the engine of the next volume's first movement.**
2. **A ROOM IS A SHAPE AND A BUILDING IS A SHAPE AND A BUILDING IS THE ONLY SHAPE IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES THAT IS NOT A PAGE, and it is the first thing anybody in this city has built that is meant to be sat in by somebody who is not from this city.** A room is gone in a day. A page travels. This does not. **The four rules of the room are on the page and they are a house style now: no table, because a table is a page you can sit at; no bench, because a bench facing a door is a seat and a seat is a place to be angry at; a step at the back with no name on it, because a step is a thing a floor has and not a thing a room offers; and nothing on the walls, because a wall in a room is the cheapest thing in this city and there is already one — and the date on the door, because a door is not a page.**
3. **ABOUT TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY PEOPLE CAN HEAR OUT OF A ROOM, A HALL, A LANDING AND A MARKET, and this city already knew that and has known it for a hundred and forty-nine days and used it four times at a roll of public business on a wall.** The instrument is four rooms and not one and it is the largest this city has and it is about four hundred years old and it disappears when the day does. **No later chapter may treat a crowd as an instrument that can be convened at will; it can be inherited, and it is inherited from something a hundred and forty-nine days old.**
