4. **A COMPACT WITH A PERSON'S NAME IN EVERY PLACE IN IT IS A DESCRIPTION OF A DISTRICT, AND IT IS JUST LONGER AND IT IS SIGNED.** A compact that is meant to travel cannot carry a person in every place, and a compact with a number in every place can be removed by an office, **and both halves of that are the finding and the batch did not resolve them.** A person standing at the junction of two systems of writing is a description of nine people, and this is now a standing rule for any instrument any of them is asked to read.
5. **A COUNT IN ONE PLACE AND AN ASSENT IN ANOTHER, AND THEY ARE NOT THE SAME PLACE, IS THE WHOLE OF WHAT A HUNDRED AND FORTY DAYS OF THIS CITY CAME TO, AND THE COST OF THE TWO LINES IS THAT NO OFFICE IN NINE HUNDRED MILES CAN CHECK EITHER OF THEM.** No later shape may put a count and an assent in one place without the chapter saying what that costs and who pays it.
6. **A REFUSAL THAT WAS NEVER ASKED FOR IS NOT A REFUSAL, AND A LIST OF REMARKS IS A LIST, AND A NUMBER NOBODY CAN CHECK IS BETTER THAN A LIST ANYBODY CAN BE ANGRY AT — with the cost stated on the page: if the number is eight on the day, nobody will ever be able to say who was not in the room.** A decision not to ask is a page. This city chose that on purpose, in a room, with a date on it.
7. **A NAME SAID OUT LOUD ONCE IN A ROOM IS A PERSON, AND A NAME ON A PAGE IS A PLACE A PERSON CAN BE FOUND IN.** The arrivals were recorded as named guests on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, and that is what *named* means in this volume: about nine people said their own names out loud, once each, in daylight, and not one of them was asked and not one of the nine names was written down by anybody anywhere. **The page with a count of nine and nobody at the top of it and the nine names in no page are both true and they disagree, and a volume may not have it both ways and a chapter may not resolve the disagreement.**
8. **A DELEGATION IS A NUMBER WITH LEGS. You can send a number and you cannot send a page, and when four comes back there will be no person in the account to be cross with.** A person who is asked whether they will go is a person on a list, and a list of who will stand in a room is a removal with a column for where the person is. **A delegation is the same page with a longer column and a worse place to be found in, and a Volume 04 chapter that writes a delegation down as a list of names has undone the best thing Volume 03 made.**
9. **THE ONE SHAPE A MAN WHO CAN READ TWO SYSTEMS OF WRITING MAY NOT BE GIVEN, RESTATED AND FINAL FOR VOLUME 03: a name, a title, a standing, a workway, a mark, a seat, a wage, a roster, a column, or any arrangement in which his reading is the price of somebody else's place.** And the sentence that goes with it: **a reading that is the price of a place is a reading that is the price of a place, and a thing that cannot be argued with because it is a day is a chair with no date on it.** He refused a Crown appointment with unlimited authority on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, and then refused a later offer that looked like the post and was not the post, in the same breath and for the same reason, and the reason was stated once and not improved on. **A later writer may not offer him a third instrument of the same family and may not offer him a better one.**
10. **A POST DOES NOT CHANGE A FORM. A POST CHANGES WHO FILLS IN THE PLACES.** And a compact is a form, and a form exists whether or not anybody will stand in it, and if he had said yes the compact would have been exactly the eleven lines it is with his voice in it instead of hers. **No later chapter may present the refusal as the cause of anything, and the volume's resolution was in the room and not in the refusal.**
11. **A MAN WITH NO MARK, ON NO ROSTER, OUTSIDE EVERY OFFICE, IS THE MOST FINDABLE PERSON IN THIS WORLD, AND THAT IS NOT A SAFETY.** He said it out loud, in front of about two hundred and forty people, after he had given the thing up and not before. **This is now a fact about him and not an opinion about him, and any chapter that needs him to be safe is a chapter that has misread him.**
12. **A THING THAT IS OPENED IN A HEARING IS A THING THE HEARING IS ABOUT.** A notary of fifty was asked, in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by a person, on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, for the first time in thirty-three days, and said no — not to being asked, and no to the opening — and the box is still shut and the strap on it is still untied and the writing round the edge of the wax has not been read by anybody in a hundred and fifty-four days. **The Void's own rule turned outward: the one instrument this city had with that office was a room, and a room is gone in a day, and a box opened in it would have made the room about a box for four hundred years.**
13. **A PERSON WHO CARRIES A COST IS A PERSON WHO CAN BE ASKED, AND THE INSTRUMENT FOR ASKING EXISTS AND IT TOOK FOUR DAYS TO BUILD: the question in the person's own words, given in writing to everybody who will be in the room four days beforehand, anybody who cannot be given it not going to be in the room, a date on a slate, and nobody asking the person whether the day suited them.** It was used once, on a Hearthguard of forty-four, on the forty-seventh day of the month after this one, after twenty-seven days, in a room, in daylight, on a day he did not choose; he said no; nobody thanked him and he asked in advance not to be. **This is the first instrument of its kind in this city and it is on the page and it works, and the count of twenty-seven days is not going to be counted again.**
14. **A WAGE PAID OUT OF SOMEONE'S OWN POCKET IS A FAVOUR AND A FAVOUR IS A THING THAT STOPS, and the page for it should carry the payer's name and not the paid man's, because a person who is owed on a page is a person with somebody, and in about four months that somebody is going to be asked a question by somebody nine hundred miles away.** The local shape is a figure on the end of an entry, a hand, and a date. This is a rule about the *page*, not about the wage, and it does not license anybody to pay a wage in a way that makes the payee findable.
15. **A PAGE ON THE BACK OF A THING NOBODY CAN TAKE DOWN IS A NEW KIND OF PROTECTION AND THERE IS NO WORD FOR IT, and the condition that makes it work is that the keeping household may take it down on a date of its own choosing and is not asked why twice.** The fourth page of a stone is on the back of it and the second page, which is wrong, is to be left wrong on purpose, **because a stone that has had a page taken down is a stone that can be corrected and a stone that can be corrected is a stone that will be corrected by whoever comes next.** A later chapter may not correct that page and may not invent a word for four.
16. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY.** Chapter 0001, Chapter 0049 (the forty-ninth day of the flood is a Monday) and Chapter 0100 (the hundredth is a Wednesday) agree with it. **Chapters 0121 to 0130 each carried a weekday two days out, which was the eleventh finding in the family that both reviews were built on, and Chapters 0131 to 0150 followed the rule. THE TEN CHAPTERS WERE CORRECTED IN THE VOLUME 03 CLOSE PHASE, one figure at a time, recomputed from day 1 and never from a neighbouring chapter or a day map, and the drift is gone: days 121 to 130 now run Wednesday, Thursday, Friday, Saturday, Sunday, Monday, Tuesday, Wednesday, Thursday, Friday.** A later writer may not follow a day map instead of day 1.
17. **THE VOID IS SPENT AND SIX ROOMS HAVE FAILED IN THE SAME PLACE AND THE SEVENTH THING THEY TRIED WAS *WHAT THE ABSENCE COST*, AND IT GOT ABOUT FOUR MINUTES, AND THE COUNT OF ROOMS ON THE PAGE IS SIX AND IS NOT BEING MADE SEVEN, BECAUSE A COUNT THAT MOVES IS A SCORE AND THIS IS NOT A SCORE.** The finding of that room is the batch's: **the only evidence anybody in four hundred miles has of what that page did is what it cost, and about a hundred and forty people paid it, and putting them in a book in order to explain an object is a description of people.** No chapter may show the page working, reproduce it, explain it, or produce a mechanism; **a city that has written the six down has failed a seventh time in a different room in the same place, and the second of those is not progress.**
18. **THE INTERFACE HAS MADE THIRTY-TWO OFFERS AND HAS NOW REGISTERED THREE REFUSALS OUT LOUD, and the third was the thirty-second, on the forty-ninth day of the month after this one, refused in about four seconds by a woman of twenty-nine who keeps a public register and who named the cost before she said it.** The count of refusals in the second book a clerk of the Registry keeps at home and has never shown to anybody is **twenty-five**, up by one, and this is the third time it has ever moved; the count at Aurel is thirteen; **the difference is twelve, and it was eleven, and it was ten for about a hundred days, and it was nine before the first registered refusal, and it has never been made smaller.** The two counts are not the same count: the twenty-five is a column in a clerk's book and the three is how many of the thirty-two things the wall has said in this city a person has said no to in a room. **Any later refusal out loud in this book must cost somebody something specific and the chapter must say what.**
19. **SIXTY SILENCES IN A ROW, SIXTY FOR SIXTY**, counted silences and not days, and a day the wall speaks on is not a silence however hard the day was. The wall spoke **once** in these twenty days, on the forty-ninth day of the month after this one, and a person refused it in about four seconds, and **that day repeats the number before it and the count does not move on it**, exactly as Chapter 0127 repeated thirty-four and Chapter 0116 repeated twenty-four. *(This is the same count as §14.10, §15.10, §16.18, §17.18, §18.18, §19.21, §20.11, §21.13 and §22.13, and all nine must be changed together. All nine carry the figure appropriate to their own batch and carry it as an amendment note in the section they amend, not as a replacement of it. **§23.19 is the count at the close of Volume 03 and is sixty, and this cross-reference said §23.13, which is the instrument for asking a person a question properly, and that was a state-file figure pointing at the wrong item and is corrected.** The whole chain was recomputed against the pages in the Volume 03 close phase: nineteen at §20.11, twenty-eight at §21.13, thirty-seven at §22.13 and sixty here, and the running count on the pages is twenty at Chapter 0111, twenty-four at 0115, twenty-four again at 0116 because the wall spoke, twenty-eight at 0120, thirty-four at 0126, thirty-four again at 0127 because the wall spoke, forty-five at 0138, forty-five again at 0139 because the wall spoke, and sixty at 0150. **Five days in Volume 03 are not silences and none of them moved the count: the fifteenth, the seventeenth, the twenty-sixth, the thirty-seventh and the forty-ninth days of the month after this one, which are days 105, 107, 116, 127 and 139, and the count is one unbroken run from twelve at Chapter 0101 to sixty at Chapter 0150 on a single base of eighty-nine.**)*
20. **THE MECHANISM BY WHICH NAMES LEAVE A PLACE IN THIS WORLD IS A FORM WITH ONE PLACE FOR A COUNT AND ONE PLACE FOR AN ASSENT, AND THEY ARE THE SAME PLACE, AND A MAN WHO WAS TAKEN GOES IN THE COUNT AND DOES NOT GO IN THE ASSENT, AND THERE IS NO COLUMN ANYWHERE IN NINE HUNDRED MILES FOR A MAN WHO WAS TAKEN.** Said out loud, in a room, in daylight, in the local language, on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, by a man with no standing, who had nine more sentences and did not say them. **This is the volume's answer and it is unpaid, and the eight names at the foot of a hundred-and-forty-year-old page were not read and the case with the question in its eighth column is on a desk nine hundred miles away and may not be answered here, intercepted, or taken back, and the reading of the number that comes back in about five weeks is not his.** No later chapter may improve on the mechanism, and no later chapter may use it to locate the eight.
21. **DATE-FORM AMENDMENT, ENTERED BY THE VOLUME 04 OUTLINE PHASE AND BINDING ON CHAPTER 0151 ONLY ONCE THE PAGE CARRIES IT. The third month is named, and the name is *the Long Month*, and the form is *the Nth day of the Long Month*, and the Nth day of it is day 155 + N, so the first day of the Long Month is day 156 and its sixty-fifth and last day is day 220.** The reason the amendment exists is in the file and is not a stylistic one: **the two month forms in this book are both sixty-five days, day 154 is the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, day 155 is its sixty-fifth and last, and nothing on a page established a sixty-sixth, so the calendar had run out** — and a volume whose resolution is a compact between two named communities cannot date that compact in a month that has no name, **and the only person in Saltwake whose job is that a thing can be certified is a notary of fifty.** The form is put on the page in prose, in a room, in daylight, with a date on it, by that notary, in **Chapter 0152, day 156, a Wednesday**, after a person who does not want to be asked has refused and the refusal has been entered. **No chapter may use a form that no page has established, and the four forms and their day ranges are now: *The Nth day of this flood* over days 1 to 40; *The Nth day of the new month* over days 26 to 90, so the Nth is day 25 + N; *The Nth day of the month after this one* over days 91 to 155, so the Nth is day 90 + N; and from day 156 *The Nth day of the Long Month*, so the Nth is day 155 + N.** Never *of this month*, *of the month*, *of next month* or *of this new month*, and never a form like *the last day of the month before this one*. ***March* is a month and is never a date number.** The day range and the per-chapter day allocation for Volume 04 are in `outline/volume-04.md` §14.1 and not here, and **the outline is a plan and the page is the fact: if a chapter's date disagrees with this item, this item is right about the form and the page is right about the day, and the conflict is entered in the batch summary rather than resolved silently.**

---

## 24. Rules established in Volume 04, Chapters 151–170

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the first twenty days of Volume 04, which is the first movement of four. §23 is unchanged, because §23.19 is the close of Volume 03 and a rule that has been closed is not reopened by a later batch.**

1. **DATE-FORM AMENDMENT, ENTERED BY CHAPTER 0152 AND BINDING ON THE WHOLE VOLUME. The fourth form is *the Nth day of the Long Month*, and the Nth day of it is day 155 + N, so the first day of the Long Month is day 156, a Wednesday, and its sixty-fifth and last day is day 220.** It is established on a page, in a room over a market, in daylight, with a date in the new form in chalk on the door, by a notary of fifty who said the cost out loud first in nine sentences and asked for it to be entered that she was going to do it anyway. **A name invented by a notary is a name an office can use, and a clerk nine hundred miles away is going to write *the Long Month* at the top of a page and nobody in that building will be able to tell whether it means the month she means.** The four forms are: the Nth day of this flood (1–40); the Nth day of the new month (26–90, N = day − 25); the Nth day of the month after this one (91–155, N = day − 90); the Nth day of the Long Month (156–220, N = day − 155). **Day 1 is a Tuesday, so a weekday is a figure and every weekday is derived by arithmetic and never from a neighbouring chapter.**

2. **A WATER GATE IS NOT OWNED AND CANNOT BE BOUGHT, AND WHAT A HOUSEHOLD HAS OF ONE IS THE DAYS ON IT** — which days of which week that household's share may be let through — **and a board, and a keeping of it, and a name in a speaker's record. The gate itself stays where it is. Nobody can take the days off a household; a household has to give them. And if it gives the days it still has the gate, and the gate will still be there in four months, and the only thing that will be different is whose turn it is.** Said once, in the local language, at a junction, in daylight. **This is the rule the volume's climax is built on: a household can only lose a gate by agreeing, which is why the midpoint is a consent and not a seizure and why the gate is recoverable.**

3. **THE REACH'S RECORD OF WHO MAY USE A WATER GATE IS A LIST OF PERSONS, AND IT HAS KEPT ONE FOR NINE YEARS, AND EVERY NAME ON IT IS ALIVE, AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY HAS EVER BEEN ANGRY ABOUT IT.** It kept water coming. **A commons keeping a list of people for good reasons is not a page about people, and a chapter may hold it without flinching and may not resolve it.** A keeper is not on the list because nobody asked her, and being asked would have meant the gate was hers.

4. **THE FOURTH CHANNEL OF THE JUNCTION AT NINE ELMS IS THE NINE ELMS GATE, OF THE REED REACH.** Named on a board in chalk at the junction, in daylight, on day 158, by the water-count, whose office it is to record who may use a water gate. **It was shut on day 112 by the fourth line of a term sheet which is in force. The quarter that was to pay for it is gone. The undertaking that was to pay for it is void and no office has been told. It stays shut for the whole of Volume 04 and no chapter of the volume opens it and no chapter explains the fourth line.**

5. **REDROOT IS A PLACE BEFORE IT IS A BODY, AND THE BODY WAS NAMED ON DAY 170 IN A ROOM WITH A DOOR, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE IN CHALK ON A DOOR, BY A PERSON WHO DID NOT WANT TO BE ASKED, AND THE MAN WHO HAD REFUSED THE APPOINTMENT WAS NOT IN THE ROOM.** Redroot Commons has no roll, no officer, no seat and no standing. **Nobody's workway is a term of it, and no party to it may be asked to give one up.** The room did not vote, because a count is a list of who said what. **A commons that can be asked is a danger and a commons that cannot be asked is a secret, and a city cannot have either without paying somebody for it, and somebody is going to pay.** Not being in the room was a protection rather than a cost for the first time in a hundred and sixty-nine days.

6. **A WAGE PAGE CARRIES THE PAYER'S NAME AND NOT THE PAID MAN'S, AND A STORE ARRIVED AT IT ON ITS OWN IN ABOUT NINE WEEKS AND NOBODY IN THIS CITY TOLD THEM.** A page is a place a person can be found in. **A wage paid out of somebody's own pocket is a favour and a favour is a thing that stops; a wage paid out of a store by people who owe it is not a favour, and stopping is a different act.** One day of a wage was paid out of a store to nine of thirty-nine men, out of a store and not out of a stall.

7. **A WORKWAY IS A PERMISSION AND NOT A TRADE, AND ITS COST IS TOLD BEFORE THE ACCEPTING, IN DAYLIGHT, WITH A DATE ON IT.** A Warden is a groundkeeper: it reinforces a claimed place, organises defence, resists involuntary displacement, it must defend the place and its residents, **and abandoning a claimed ground is a matter of the holder's convenience.** There is no other clause and no second page. **Whether the offer is a breach of a term of the trial licence is a question about a term, and the only office that can answer is nine hundred miles away, and the next time this city can put anything at all in front of that office is day 189 — so the question is real, correct, unanswered and beside the point, and the volume's whole argument about forms is that a real correct unanswered question is worth nothing to a family.** **A duty to a place is the only wage anybody in this world has ever been able to offer anybody.**

8. **THE MONEY IN THE SETTLEMENT IS SALT, BECAUSE SALT IS THE ONLY PRODUCT IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES A PERSON CAN SELL TO SOMEBODY NINE HUNDRED MILES AWAY.** One buyer. A causeway of four miles that took forty days, built by the settlement and not by the Reach, and no other way out of the Reach flats with a load on it. Four hundred and forty racks. About nine hundred measures a week. **A settlement that needs water has to be given it, and being given it is a thing that can be asked for, and a gate is not a thing anybody can own.**

9. **THE PAGES AND THE INTERFACES ADRIAN NOW HOLDS ARE TWO, AND NEITHER IS A POWER, A POST, A SEAT, A ROSTER OR A COLUMN, AND NOBODY CAN BE WRITTEN TO FOR HAVING EITHER.** (a) **A share with the post taken out** — about eleven lines, no date, no place, nothing in it anybody could be obliged to do, and no way to revoke it, because there is nothing to take. **A share with no date on it is a share that goes on for as long as nobody objects, and it is not a lease, because a lease has an end on it and a date where the end is, and nobody is going to be annoyed, because you pay. It is a queue.** (b) **A name he did not choose: *the witness*,** given in nine sentences, in daylight, in a room, in the plain language of an offer. **A NAME SAID OUT LOUD THAT IS A PLACE A PERSON CAN BE FOUND IN — the first time in this book that the two halves of that rule have come apart, and being findable is not a penalty for being real. It is the price of it.**

10. **A REFUSAL IS A PAGE AND A DECISION NOT TO DO SOMETHING IS ALSO A PAGE, AND A MAN WHO HAS PUT A REFUSAL ON A PAGE FOR A HUNDRED AND SIXTY-FOUR DAYS HAS NEVER ONCE PUT A DELAY ON ONE.** Entered on day 164, in his own hand, with the date on it, in a gateway, in daylight: **he is going to do nothing about the quarter for twenty-five days on purpose, and the delay is the length of the quarter exactly, and it is going to cost somebody a gate, and he cannot improve on it because it is not a shape, it is a date.** The quarter runs out on **day 189, a Monday**, thirty-five days after the hearing on day 154. **It is the only clock in the book running towards something and this volume does not replace it.**

11. **A QUESTION ASKED PROPERLY IS AN INSTRUMENT AND IT TOOK FOUR DAYS TO BUILD, AND A MAN WHO HAS GIVEN UP THE READING HAS NOW TURNED IT ON HIMSELF, AND THE ANSWER WAS ONE WORD AND THE WORD WAS NOT A NOUN.** The rule he set himself — that he is not to ask one person one question — was broken on day 165, with the date on it, and he entered that he broke it. **Doing nothing is a working and this one has no residue anybody can name, and the finding of a day of doing nothing on purpose is that there is nothing to do that he is the one to do, which is a different thing from there being nothing to do.**

12. **THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S SILENCES STANDS AT SEVENTY-EIGHT AND THE WALL SPOKE ON DAY 173 AND REPEATED THE NUMBER BEFORE IT AND THE COUNT DID NOT MOVE.** Sixty-one on day 155, seventy-one on day 165, seventy-eight on day 173. **A day the wall says something on is not a silence however hard the day was.**

13. **THE INTERFACE HAS MADE AN OFFER THAT WAS REFUSED IN ABOUT FOUR SECONDS AT A COST NAMED OUT LOUD BY A PERSON IN THE ROOM, AND THE PANEL THAT CAME UP SAID: any correct figure may be checked by a person; the person who checks it answers for the check and not for the figure; nothing is owed, nothing is given; it can be done in a room in daylight.** **A correct figure anybody can check is the instrument this city spent three volumes failing to build, and it is a panel and not an office, and it asks nothing and gives nothing.**

14. **REDROOT IS NOT ON THE CHAPTER 0010 LIST OF THE HAMLETS ON THE SHARED GATES, AND THE WATER-COUNT HAS KNOWN THAT SINCE THE TENTH DAY OF THIS FLOOD AND HAS NEVER ONCE ASKED WHY.** The name was said out loud once, at a gate, in daylight, on a board and not on a page, **because a board is a thing and a page is a place a person can be found in, and it was said once and no more.** The answer to why is that a list is a list.

15. **THE WORD *ORDER* HAS BEEN ARRIVED AT NINE TIMES IN THIS BOOK BY A MAN FROM ANOTHER WORLD AND BY A WOMAN OF FIFTY-EIGHT WHO CANNOT READ, AND NEITHER OF THEM HAS EVER HEARD OF THE OTHER, AND THE FIRST TIME THE TWO HAVE BEEN IN A ROOM IS CHAPTER 0169, AND NO CHARACTER IN THAT CHAPTER KNOWS IT.** Jonah Pike is right about eight sentences out of nine and his tenth part is always a local woman's word for a social fact.

---

## 25. Rules established in Volume 04, Chapters 171–190

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the second twenty days of Volume 04. §23 is not changed, because §23.19 is the close of Volume 03, and §24 is not changed, because §24 is the close of the first twenty chapters of this volume. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-04-batch-0002.md`.**

1. **A HOUSEHOLD CAN BE ASKED FOR THE DAYS ON A GATE AND IT SAYS YES, AND THE GATE DOES NOT GO ANYWHERE.** Day 179, the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month, in a kitchen at Redroot, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, **about nine people in the room, no hall, no landing and no market under it.** The cost was read out **first, in full, in the words of the person offering it**, and nobody improved on it. The household said yes in one sentence. **There was no vote, because a count is a list.** A boy of eight was not in the room and was not asked. **A gate is not owned and the only way a household loses one is by agreeing, and giving the days back means the gate never went anywhere** — that is the volume's engine, it was said once by the water-count in Chapter 0154, said again in full by the keeper of the Redroot gate in a kitchen on day 181, and **may not be improved on and may not be said as a discovery a second time.**
2. **THE DAYS ARE THE HOUSEHOLD'S AND ONLY THE HOUSEHOLD CAN GIVE THEM BACK, AND A QUESTION PUT TO ANY OTHER BODY IS A QUESTION THAT BODY HAS TO SEND BACK TO THE HOUSEHOLD.** Said out loud in a room over a market in front of about two hundred and forty people on day 194. **The instrument for the recovery of a gate is therefore not a document and not a person and not an office: it is a community's own four-hundred-year-old rule that every household affected by a change must be heard, and a body that floats has to be poled to where the people are. A chapter may not build a better instrument than that and may not name it.**
3. **THE QUARTER RAN OUT ON DAY 189, A MONDAY, THIRTY-FIVE DAYS AFTER THE HEARING ON DAY 154, AND A THING THAT WAS GOING TO ARRIVE ON THAT DAY DID NOT ARRIVE ON THAT DAY.** There is no road in this world that reaches that office, nothing can be sent and nothing could be prepared. **The finding of the day is nine words long and belongs to a man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board: a thing that was going to arrive on a day and did not is a different kind of information from a thing that arrives.** *This was the only clock in the book running towards something and it has run out and it is not replaced.*
4. **WHAT CAME BACK IS THE FIFTEENTH SHAPE, DATED THE THIRTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 190, A TUESDAY: A DISTRICT FLOOD RETURN OF FOUR LINES AND A HEADING, MADE BY A DISTRICT WATERBOARD IN THE ORDINARY COURSE, DATED THE TWENTY-FOURTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, BROUGHT UP THE REACH ROAD IN A CART THAT WAS GOING SOMEWHERE ELSE.** It has no person's name on it anywhere. **It agrees with the twelfth shape, which is nailed under oiled cloth in a converted salt store under a lock chapel in this city and cannot be taken down, and the two are forty-five days apart. Two correct documents that agree are a different object from either of them: one correct document is a thing that can be argued with and two are a thing that can only be agreed about.** The reading of that number is not Adrian's, is not the woman of twenty-four's and is not the man of about thirty-nine's. **It is neither good news nor bad news and no word of either kind is in the room. Nobody in four hundred miles can act on it and nobody in nine hundred miles can be asked anything about it before the next quarter, and the next quarter is not a date anybody in this city holds.** It is the fifteenth shape and it is not the twelfth and it is not the ninth and it is not the eleventh.
5. **A RETURN IS NOT A CHANGE, AND A THING THAT IS NOT A CHANGE IS THE MOST DANGEROUS KIND OF THING THERE IS.** A district, a number, and a line that says the district assented. No room, no person, nobody to say no, correct, unhurried, four times a year, and the count and the assent are the same number they were the last time. **The Reach's own rule — every household affected by a change must be heard — does not reach a quarterly return, and that is the correct outcome, and a chapter may not find it comfortable and may not fill the hole it does not make.**
6. **A COST READ OUT IN FIVE ROOMS GETS SHORTER EVERY TIME, AND THE PERSON READING IT IS THE ONE WHO SHORTENS IT, AND HE DID NOT DECIDE TO AND CANNOT FIND THE ROOM WHERE HE DID.** Said out loud in a kitchen on day 176, before anybody asked him, by the man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom. **Every word of the cost is true and always was; the reader is the variable.** There is no column anywhere in four hundred or nine hundred miles for a person who notices this about himself and cannot be removed for it and cannot be paid for it and does not want to be. **A later chapter may not use this to argue that the cost was a lie. The cost is true and it was read five times.**
7. **NINE DAYS OF A WEEK IS NOT A WEEK.** A rota is seven days, the two extra days have to sit somewhere, and the somewhere is a person, and a settlement that would put a ninth day at the end of a week without reading the cost of it out in a room first stopped instead. **A rota is the instrument a settlement actually runs on and it is a page, and it is a page in four hands, and a thing copied twice is two things.**
8. **A SETTLEMENT CAN BE RUN ON A NOUN.** A man of about forty-two found out in a yard on day 185 that he had been running a settlement on the word *season* for nine weeks, and that a season is not a date, and that the only date in four hundred miles is the fortieth day of the Long Month, which is a Sunday, which he chose because about a hundred and forty people are paid on a Tuesday. **A day is a thing a household loses and a word is a thing a household is left holding, and the finding of the thirty-ninth day of the Long Month is that a date nobody chose is the only instrument a body that floats can be poled to.**
9. **THE ONLY INSTRUMENT A MAN WITH NO STANDING HAS IS A QUESTION, AND IT BREAKS ON A BODY.** The instrument — the question in the person's own words, given in writing to everybody who will be in the room four days beforehand, anybody who cannot be given it not going to be in the room, nobody asking the person whether the day suits them — was used on a Hearthguard of forty-four on a day he did not choose, on a woman of about thirty-six in a gateway, and on a meeting-ground. **The third use produced the finding: a question given in writing to about nine people four days beforehand cannot be changed afterwards, and the first thing this city's own instrument did was stop its author, and one person who could not be given the question was not going to be in the room and said why in her own words.** And **a man of about thirty-two stood at the back of a room for about four hours on day 194 and did not answer it, and that is the first thing he has done in a hundred and ninety-four days that he has not refused, and it is not a stage and it is not a title and there is no column for it.**
10. **A PERSON WHO IS FINDABLE CAN BE SENT INTO ANY ROOM AND CANNOT BE SENT TO A GATE, AND THE WALL SAID IT ON DAY 191 IN FOUR SENTENCES AND IN PLAIN LANGUAGE AND ASKED FOR NOTHING.** The panel, refused before it had finished going away: *the days on a gate can be given back by the household alone; no other party may do it; a person who is findable can be sent into any room; a person who is findable cannot be sent to a gate; nothing is owed, nothing is given, it can be done in a room in daylight.* **The cost of the refusal is the name he did not choose, given in nine sentences on day 173, and the finding is that being findable is the price of being real and is also the reason he cannot be the one who goes to the gate.** It is not a power, a post, a seat, a wage, a roster or a column, and nobody can be written to for having it.
11. **A COST MAY BE SAID OUT LOUD IN A ROOM BY THE PERSON PAYING IT AND NOBODY MAY NAME A PRICE FIRST AND NOBODY MAY IMPROVE ON IT, and this is the only instrument anybody has built in this flood that nobody can refuse, because a cost is a thing a person has already said and a person cannot unsay it.** Four sentences long, built in about nine minutes, in a room in daylight with a date on the door. **A cost is a promise. It is not a table and it is not a form and it does not go anywhere and nobody can check it, and a person who says a thing is nothing in front of about nine people has said it out loud and cannot unsay it.**
12. **A COALITION SPLITS BY PEOPLE SAYING OUT LOUND WHAT THEY WILL NOT DO, AND A COUNT OF THAT IS A ROLL, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO COULD HAVE WRITTEN IT DOWN DECLINED TO AND SAID WHY.** People who want a wage and people who want a name, and neither side is wrong. **No figure is on any page and no chapter before day 198 may state either figure.**
13. **A GATE IS NOT A ROAD AND A ROAD IS NOT A GATE, and the only instrument in four hundred miles that can stop a road is the ground it is on, and the ground belongs to about nine households and a woman of thirty-eight whose days can be given back.** Said in a yard on day 182 by a man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board. **A causeway of four miles, four feet wide at the top, built in forty days by about forty people who were not from here, on ground taken in the seventh week of this flood without asking, and it is the only way out of the flats with a load on it, and no chapter in this volume may call a road a gate or a gate a road.**
14. **A WAGE WENT THE OTHER WAY, ONCE, ON DAY 187, and the finding is that the thing which has not happened in a hundred and eighty-seven days is a wage paid by the settlement to people on the Reach, and nobody decided it and nobody has written it down, and in about four months a clerk is going to find that about a hundred and forty people who are paid a wage on a day were on a list they were not put on, and there is no instrument that takes a person off a list they were not put on.** Two pairs of boots were a favour and a favour is a thing that stops.
15. **THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S SILENCES IS NINETY-FIVE AND THE WALL SPOKE ON DAY 191 AND REPEATED THE NUMBER BEFORE IT AND THE COUNT DID NOT MOVE.** Seventy-eight on day 174, and the running figure is one addition for each day the wall says nothing, so days 174 to 190 are seventeen additions and the figure is ninety-five at day 190. **It was paid for at three points in the twenty chapters and not more: ninety on day 185, ninety-four on day 189, and ninety-five on day 191, which repeats.** A day the wall says something on is not a silence however hard the day was. **There have been thirty-four things the wall has said in this city and five of them have been refused out loud, and this is the second time it has put two halves of the same fact in one field, and the first was on day 173.**
16. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS DERIVED FROM AN ANCHOR AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER.** The days the quarter has left are 189 less the day. The days the thirty-nine men under the near gate have gone unpaid are the day less eighty-five, the first of March being the eighty-fifth day of this flood: ninety at Chapter 0171, ninety-four at Chapter 0175, a hundred and four at Chapter 0185, a hundred and nine at Chapter 0190. The fourth channel at the Nine Elms junction was shut on day 112 and the junction has not received water since day 69: seventy-one days shut and a hundred and fourteen days without water at Chapter 0179. The Long Month day number is the chapter's own day less 155. **A case with a question in its eighth column went down this river on the ninety-second day of this flood and has been on a desk nine hundred miles away since: ninety-seven days at Chapter 0185.** *The case had been on a desk a hundred and forty-odd days is not a figure any page supports and is not on one; the derived figure is ninety-seven and a later writer must use the derived figure.*
17. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0171 TO 0190 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1.** Twenty opening weekdays and every interior weekday word in the twenty were checked one at a time. **Day 194 is a Saturday and carries two chapters, and the second of them is a Saturday, and there is no second day and no second weekday in the volume.**
18. **NOTHING IN THESE TWENTY CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a date that came back, a gate that is still there, a name that turned out to be the reason he cannot be the one who goes to the gate, a cost said out loud in a room by about nine people, a question in four sentences that is on its way to the wrong body, and the discovery that this city's own instrument stops its author. **Adrian remains Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a third time, the other world is not named once in the twenty chapters, and no chapter offered him a workway and he asked for none.**

---

## 26. Rules established in Volume 04, Chapters 191–200

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the last ten days of Volume 04, which is the volume's last movement. §23 is not changed, because §23.19 is the close of Volume 03, and §24 is not changed, because §24 is the close of the first twenty chapters of this volume, and §25 is not changed, because §25 is the close of the second twenty. The volume's close record is `state/volume-04-close.md` and the batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-04-batch-0003.md`.**

1. **A MEETING-GROUND IS A RAFT WITH A ROOF ON IT AND NINE FEET OF STANDING ROOM AND A RAIL, AND IT CANNOT BE SUMMONED, AND A PLACE CAN BE ARGUED WITH AND A DATE CANNOT BECAUSE THERE IS NOBODY AT A DATE.** It was poled eleven miles up the Reach on the fortieth day of the Long Month, day 195, in four hours, two men with poles and a woman with a line, and it was stuck once in a place where the channel narrows and was got off in about twenty minutes. **It is the only instrument that can be poled to a date nobody chose, and there was no other date in four hundred miles that month it could have been poled to.** It holds no days on any gate and it said so in a room on the same day.

2. **THE RULE A BODY THAT FLOATS WORKS BY IS FOUR HUNDRED YEARS OLD, HAS NO NAME ON IT, NOBODY IN EITHER OF THE LAST TWO CHAPTERS NAMED IT, AND IT SAYS THAT EVERY HOUSEHOLD AFFECTED BY A CHANGE MUST BE HEARD.** It has no column in it for which household that is. **The body that has to apply it will not guess, will not ask nine people one at a time in front of the other eight, and will not work round the houses until one of them stops talking, and the reason is in his own mouth and against himself: a raft that names a household is a body doing the thing a man who has been refused by name four times in four years has refused to do for four years, because a description is a removal with a column for where the person is.** About nine people on a flat then worked out for themselves which of them they were, for about two hours, and it was not a vote and there was no count and no hand went up. **No chapter may name the rule, build a better one, or make a person rather than a body do it.**

3. **THE DAYS WENT BACK ON THE FORTY-FIRST DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 196, IN DAYLIGHT, ON A FLAT, WITH ABOUT NINE PEOPLE ON IT, AND NOBODY ASKED THE HOUSEHOLD.** The household heard itself and spoke first, unasked, and gave all nine days of a week back, for the rest of the season, starting then. **The instrument is not the door and not the rail: the same household that gave the days in a kitchen with a date in chalk on a door gave them back on a flat with a date in chalk on the rail of a board, in a fifth hand, and the volume's one sentence was not said again and *a gate is not owned* stands at zero in all fifty chapters of Volume 04.** What is on the page is the consequence and not the definition: she has lost nine days of a week and she still has the cut, and the board, and a boy of eight, and a year of being angry with seventeen days of it gone.

4. **A WATER GATE'S DAYS STOP A ROAD BECAUSE THE RACKS STAND IN THE CHANNEL AND THE CHANNEL TAKES ITS WATER FROM A GATE, AND A RACK THAT HAS NOT BEEN WORKED DOES NOT COME OFF.** About nine people have been putting that water down one channel on nine days a week since the seventh week of this flood, and the settlement bought those nine days on the twenty-fourth of the Long Month in a kitchen, and the water stopped on the forty-second. **This mechanism is on the page in Chapter 0195 and it is not in the outline, and a later writer may use it and may not reinvent it and may not improve on it.** Four hundred and forty racks, about nine hundred measures a week, and a channel with no water in it will have nothing in it that a rack can go into in about four weeks.

5. **NOBODY SHUT ANYTHING.** A man of forty-two refused, in front of about nine people, to let anybody in this city call it a bar, because a bar is a person standing in the way and there was not one. **The loss falls on the settlement and not on the Reach, and that sentence is the reason nine people on a flat were able to do it in daylight: the whole of what was going to be lost was his and not theirs, and if it had been theirs the man who says it does not know what would have happened and is not going to pretend to.** The settlement's ground under the causeway was taken in the seventh week without asking; the racks are its; the people on its rota are nine; the salt is its until it is somebody's and it is nobody's.

6. **A WAGE THAT STOPS IS NOT A FAVOUR THAT STOPS.** A favour is a thing somebody does out of what they happen to have and a wage is a thing somebody promised, and taking a promised thing back is a different act and it has a person in it. Said in a yard nine miles up the road on the forty-second day of the Long Month. **The thirty-nine men under the near gate are a hundred and fifteen days unpaid on the forty-fifth, counted from the first of March, which is the eighty-fifth day of this flood, and that is a different debt and it did not change this week.**

7. **THE TRESTLE COUNT IS ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-EIGHT ON THE FORTY-THIRD DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 198, COUNTED ONE A DAY FROM THE FORTIETH DAY OF THIS FLOOD AND FROM NOTHING ELSE.** It was one hundred and fourteen on the sixty-fourth day of the month after this one, day 154, and eighty-nine on the thirty-ninth day of the month after this one, day 129, and **forty-four days separate the hearing from the room and there are forty-four in the difference.** The man who keeps it writes the number and dates it, one line a week, and the number is not a week's worth; he has never known what a day of it counts and has never pretended to and has never been asked in four hundred days. **It was spoken zero times in Volume 03, it had never been read out before Chapter 0196, and it is on a wall in the room it was read in, in a column with a date at the top of it, in a hand that is not his.** Nobody in this city can find out what a day of it means and nobody can stop it. **The line in the same book that is not a number has not been sent, there is no route in this world that a letter can travel, no office in nine hundred miles knows it exists, he has still not mentioned it, and no chapter of Volume 04 had him mention it.**

8. **A PERSON WHO IS NOT FINDABLE CANNOT BE CHECKED AND A PERSON WHO IS FINDABLE IS A PLACE A PERSON CAN BE FOUND IN, AND THE MAN KEEPING THE COUNT IS NEITHER, AND THE THREE ARE THREE FACTS AND NOT ONE FINDING.** Said in three sentences in a room on the forty-third day of the Long Month, and a woman of about twenty-four wrote all three down separately and did not join them up, because the day this city joins them up is the day it has a column. **Being findable is the price of being real and is not the penalty for it, and it is also the reason he cannot be sent to a gate, and on the forty-third day it cost him the trestle count: he was asked whether the day suited him, and said no, and said out loud that he would never know who checked a number he was not in the room to hear.**

9. **A CORRECT NUMBER IN THE ORDINARY COURSE GOES ON A WALL, AND A CORRECT PAGE IS A PAGE NOBODY CAN ARGUE WITH, AND A PAGE NOBODY CAN ARGUE WITH IS A PAGE NOBODY CAN STOP.** The column went up in the room it was read in, in chalk, by a man copying figures for the first time in his life, and about four people in that room had wanted it not to. **A woman of about thirty-four said in the same room that a correct page nobody can check is the only instrument this city has and that it is the most dangerous sentence anybody has said in this flood, and a man of forty-two said it out loud first and would not unsay it, and neither of them took it back and a chapter may not argue that the number can be taken down.**

10. **A COALITION SPLIT INTO NINE AND EIGHT OUT LOUD IN A YARD, WITH NO ROLL, NO COUNT AND NO VOTE, AND THE ONLY PERSON WHO COULD HAVE WRITTEN IT DOWN DECLINED AGAIN IN NINE WORDS.** The split is between people who want a wage and people who want a name and neither side is wrong. **The finding is not the split: eight of them read a number they could not understand and decided that the man who can get forty people a week out of a city that cannot get one out is the safer answer to it, and about nine of the sixteen had not realised that they had decided anything.** The nine are owed something for this and will not get it, and the nine have not heard that about four hundred and forty people on the flat cannot be reached.

11. **THE SIXTEENTH SHAPE IS DATED THE FORTY-FIFTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 200, A FRIDAY, AND IT IS ELEVEN LINES AND IT IS LOCAL.** The ground stays with the households and no land right of any kind is transferred; no workway is a term of anything and no party may be required to surrender one; the labour is shared and posted and the posting is bound to the compact and has a date on it and a price under every line; nobody may be bought out of a refusal and a refusal requires no reason and is not asked for twice; **the fourth line cannot be administered and is in the compact in the words of the man of about thirty-nine who wrote it against it, with his name and the date on it, because the Commons' own rule requires every household affected by a change to be heard and a man who has argued against them since the seventh week of this flood, which is day 49 and a hundred and fifty-one days before this one, is a household affected by a change**; the salt is a common and not a product; the road is open to everybody on a posted day; the store on the upper road is on the same posting and its queue is on the same posting and it takes its place on it and not above it; **the ninth line is a person and it is *a person may be found***; a share taken under it is on the same posting as every other share and holds no land right and no workway; and it has a date on it and the two bodies that made it are the Redroot Commons and the Reed Commons and neither can be ended by anybody who is not a party to it.

12. **THE NINTH LINE IS A PERSON MAY BE FOUND, AND IT IS NOT THE FIFTH LINE OF THE COMPACT OF ELEVEN LINES, AND THE TWO MAY NOT BE CONFUSED.** The eleventh shape's fifth line is a guest who may ask to stop being a guest and the asking is the whole of it. **A document that can be ended by the asking of a person and a document that requires a person to be findable are not the same instrument and neither one is a copy of the other, and the compact of eleven lines is not ended and is not touched and was named aloud in a room on the forty-fifth of the Long Month and distinguished from this one in five sentences that the woman of twenty-nine has now given four times and will not improve.** A commons that cannot be found cannot be a party to anything and cannot be answered for, and **nobody in this volume may be a party to a compact and unfindable, and that is the compact's cost and it is a person.**

13. **BEING FINDABLE IS THE PRICE OF BEING REAL AND NOT THE PENALTY FOR IT, AND A WOMAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR PAID IT ON THE FORTY-FOURTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AFTER NINE YEARS OF NOT PAYING IT.** She named the price before she said yes, in her own words: nobody in this city has known what she looks like while she is arguing, and from that day there is a date and a stall and a name on a page and anybody with a copy can wait at that wharf and she will be the one who comes out. **She asked for it to be entered that she said no for nine years and that this is the yes and that the two are the same person and that she is not a different woman today, and she asked in advance not to be thanked, and nobody thanked her.** Her name is on the front of the sixteenth shape and it is said once in a room in daylight and **it is deliberately not printed on any page, and `state/batch-summaries/volume-04-batch-0003.md` §8 is binding on every phase that reads this section: a later writer may not print it, may not be given it, and may not have a person in a room say it.**

14. **A POSTING IS A PAGE WITH A DATE ON IT AND A PRICE UNDER EVERY LINE, AND A RIGHT OF REFUSAL THAT CANNOT BE BOUGHT OUT IS ON IT BECAUSE THAT IS THE THIRD SENTENCE OF THE OPEN HAND, AND IT CANNOT BE ADMINISTERED, AND THE MAN WHO SAYS SO IS IN IT.** Nobody has run the compact for a day, nobody has tested the store's queue being on the same posting as everybody else's, and nobody in this city knows how an unbought refusal is administered except the man who says it cannot be. **The delegation of four people and a question has not left, nobody has been asked whether they will go, and *to open nothing* is the woman of twenty-nine's sentence and was said out loud by her in a room on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month, and this compact is not that document and does not end it and does not touch it.**

15. **THE COUNT OF THIS CITY'S SILENCES IS NINETY-EIGHT ON THE THIRTY-NINTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND ONE HUNDRED AND FOUR AT DAY 200, AND BOTH ARE DERIVED.** The wall said nothing after the thirty-sixth day of the Long Month, so days 192, 193 and 194 are three additions on ninety-five, and days 195 to 200 are six more. **It was paid for once in ten chapters, in a mouth, on the thirty-ninth, and a girl of seventeen refused to say her own count in a room on the forty-third and gave the reason, which is that the two counts are the only two numbers in this city and she is the only person who has both of them.** A day the wall says something on is not a silence however hard the day was. **There have been thirty-four things the wall has said in this city and five have been refused out loud, and this batch had no panel at all.**

16. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS DERIVED FROM AN ANCHOR AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER.** The Long Month day number is the chapter's own day less 155. The days the thirty-nine men have gone unpaid are the day less eighty-five: a hundred and twelve at Chapter 0195 and a hundred and fifteen at Chapter 0200. The trestle count is the day less forty. The causeway took forty days and is four miles long and four feet wide at the top. The cost column's one paid field is the thirteenth day of the Long Month, day 168, and the column did not move, and it is thirty days later at Chapter 0197. **The interval since the seventh week of this flood, day 49, is a hundred and forty-five days on the thirty-ninth of the Long Month and it is the only figure in this volume that was carried forward wrongly: it stood in twenty places across the last ten chapters and every one of them was repaired to the anchor, and the anchor is the store and the settlement opening in the seventh week.**

17. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 191 TO 200 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1.** Thirty-one interior weekday references were extracted by script and read against the day each one names. **Day 194 is a Saturday and carries two chapters, and the second of them is a Saturday, and there is no second day and no second weekday in the volume. Day 200 is a Friday and carries two chapters and both of them are Fridays.**

18. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a share, a name he did not choose, a month, a date, a gate that came back, a woman who went on a page on purpose, and a number on a wall. **Adrian remains Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a third time, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, and no chapter offered him a workway and he asked for none.**

---

## 27. Rules established in Volume 05, Chapters 201–210

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the first ten days of Volume 05, which is the first movement. §23 is not changed, because §23.19 is the close of Volume 03; §24 is not changed, because §24 is the close of the first twenty chapters of Volume 04; §25 is not changed; and §26 is not changed, because §26 is the close of Volume 04. The volume's close record is `state/volume-04-close.md` and this batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-05-batch-0001.md`.**

1. **THE COMPACT'S ELEVENTH LINE IS THE POSTING, AND THE POSTING WENT UP ON THE FORTY-SIXTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 201, ON A WALL OF THE STORE ON THE UPPER ROAD, IN CHALK, AT ABOUT THE SEVENTH HOUR OF THE MORNING.** It has a date at the top of it and a price under every line. **The prices are in words: a sack, a place at a fire, and a day of the queue.** No figure is under any of them, and a man of about twenty-nine from the settlement's workroom said in the yard on the day it went up that a page with a price under every line and no figure under any price is a list of intentions, and about four people in that yard agreed with him. **It is on the same wall as the store's own board and about four people in that yard read the posting and did not read the board.** Nobody has run it for a day.

2. **THE STORE AND ITS QUEUE ARE THE NINTH AND LAST LINE OF THAT POSTING, IN THE HAND OF THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL, AND HE PUT IT THERE ON PURPOSE.** He wrote the nine sentences into a page in a room over a market in daylight with a date on the door on the forty-fourth day of the Long Month, day 199, a Thursday; the compact was read out on the forty-fifth, day 200, a Friday, and the posting was bound to it; and it went on a wall on the forty-sixth, day 201, a Saturday. **A queue is a line that nobody decided and a posting is a line that somebody decided, and both of those sentences are on the page in a mouth on the forty-ninth day of the Long Month, day 204, in a yard, in daylight.** From the Saturday the only functioning way out of this world is ninth on a page, and a man of about forty-two did not write it and put a share on it that is on the same page as about nine hundred other things. **A chapter may not take that line off the wall and a chapter may not move it.**

3. **THE POSTING DOES NOT SAY WHOSE LABOUR IT IS, AND ABOUT FOUR PEOPLE IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES WORKED THAT OUT ON THE FIFTY-FIRST DAY OF THE LONG MONTH.** The labour is shared and posted and the posting is bound to a compact whose two bodies are the Redroot Commons and the Reed Commons, and there is not one name on the wall and no line of it is marked as belonging to either body. **A man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board refused a line of it on the same day and could not afterwards tell anybody which line he had refused, and about four people in that yard noticed that he did not know whose line it was either.** No name was written on the posting and no line was crossed off, and no chapter of this batch may supply either.

4. **A REFUSAL THAT CANNOT BE ADMINISTERED CANNOT BE RECORDED, AND A REFUSAL THAT CANNOT BE RECORDED CANNOT BE COUNTED, AND A POSTING WITH A RIGHT OF REFUSAL ON IT HAS NO LINE FOR A NUMBER OF REFUSALS.** A woman of about twenty-four picked a piece of chalk off the ledge under that wall on the fifty-first day of the Long Month, put it in her hand, and put it back down, and nobody wrote it down, and the only instrument left is about nine people remembering a thing. **A refusal requires no reason and is not asked for twice, and in the first refusal of the compact nobody in the room asked, and it is entered in three places that nobody wanted to.** *This does not soften the fourth line, which cannot be administered, and the two are not the same finding.*

5. **A RIGHT OF REFUSAL THAT CANNOT BE BOUGHT OUT IS A RIGHT OF REFUSAL THAT CANNOT BE AFFORDED, AND A POSTED DAY PAYS A SACK, A PLACE AT A FIRE, AND A DAY OF THE QUEUE, AND A PERSON WHO SAYS NO DOES NOT GET THEM.** Said in a mouth in a yard on the fifty-first day of the Long Month, day 206, and about nine people in this city can afford to refuse a posted day and about four hundred and thirty-one cannot. **It is a hole in the fourth line and it is not the same hole as the one in the rule of the Reach, and a man of about thirty-nine who is a party to the compact found it on the day he used the right and has entered that he found it by saying no, and about four people in that yard had been waiting five days for somebody to use the right so that they could watch what it does.** No chapter may fill either hole and no chapter may say the two are the same hole.

6. **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY PEOPLE WORK SALT ON THE FLATS AND NINE OF THEM ARE ON THE SETTLEMENT'S ROTA AND THE REST HAVE NEVER BEEN ON IT, AND THE POSTING HAS NO LINE FOR ANY OF THEM.** The answer given in a market and on a causeway and in a yard across three days is the same answer and it is a question back: *whose household are you.* **A shared thing gets shared between two bodies, and a person who has never answered to either of them is not on the page, and about four hundred and thirty-one people are going to read that page in about four days and work out exactly that, and about nine people in this city can name the four of them who will work it out first and will not.** The person who asked the question in the fourth household on the road said the only question anybody has asked her she can answer, which is which side of a channel is whose, and she has said since the twenty-first day of the Long Month that a day of a wage is not a wage. *Open and unresolved and Volume 05 may not write a line for them.*

7. **THE SEVENTH LINE SAYS THE ROAD IS OPEN TO EVERYBODY ON A POSTED DAY, AND IT IS THE ONLY ONE OF THE ELEVEN THAT SAYS ANYTHING ABOUT A ROAD, AND NOBODY IN THE CITY KNOWS WHICH ROAD.** About four people in this city have worked it out since and have not said it out loud. There is one road anybody in this city has built: it is four miles, it took forty days, about forty people who were not from here built it, and the ground it stands on was taken in the seventh week of this flood without asking, and the man who built it is a party to the compact. **A man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall has said in writing in a yard that he is not going to ask him, and that nobody is going to, and a woman of about fifty-six who keeps a shop four streets down from a market has said that about four people in this city have already worked it out and have told each other, and that is all she is going to say about it.** *Open. No chapter of this batch asked him and no chapter may.*

8. **A COPY OF A PAGE TRAVELS FURTHER AND FASTER THAN A PAGE, AND A RUMOUR TRAVELS FURTHER STILL, AND NEITHER OF THEM HAS A COLUMN.** A man of about forty-four who keeps a stall copied the front of the sixteenth shape onto the back of a slate on a table in a market at about the fourth hour of the afternoon on the forty-fifth day of the Long Month, day 200, because he thought a page ought to be in two places, and he put it down on the boards of a stall by a wharf on the fifty-third day of the Long Month, day 208, and about four hundred people who read a wall nine miles up the road that week trace back to that table and not to the wharf. **On the same day a second man came and stood in front of that stall with nothing in his hands, having heard about the name from about four people who had heard it from about four other people, and the woman of about thirty-four who keeps that stall said out loud that a page is a place a person can be found in and that it is the small half, and that about four people telling about four other people in about eight days is the half that has no page and no office nine hundred miles away that can stop it.** *A copy of a page is a page. A rumour is not a page and is not a document and is not on a page, and no chapter may make it one.*

9. **BEING FINDABLE IS THE PRICE OF BEING REAL AND IT IS THE SMALL HALF, AND A NAME SAID OUT LOUD ONCE IN A ROOM IN DAYLIGHT WITH A DATE ON THE DOOR IS THE INSTRUMENT THIS CITY HAS BEEN USING SINCE THE SEVENTH WEEK OF THIS FLOOD, AND NOBODY CAN TAKE A WORD OUT OF A ROOM.** Adrian Vale said his own name out loud in that form in a room over a market on the fifty-fourth day of the Long Month, day 209, a Sunday, at about the eighth hour of the morning, in the local language, in front of about nine people and about two hundred and forty who could hear, and a woman of about twenty-four had her hand on a slate and did not put it down, and about nine people in that hall will say the name if anybody asks them and it is in no book. **He was fetched by that name at about the seventh hour of the morning that day by a woman who was not sent by anybody, and he answered to it in about a second, and he has entered that he answered to it quickly.** About nine people in this city can now fetch him. **He could not answer the question he was fetched for, which was whether the ninth line works, and he said so in four sentences and would not improve on them, and about four people in that room wanted a better sentence and about nine of them did not.** *It is not a power, a post, a seat, a wage, a roster or a column. Nobody in this batch offered him any of them and he asked for none, and a man of about thirty-four who keeps a stall asked for that to be put in the record and not said out loud again, because every time it is said out loud in a room it sounds like a thing being withheld from him.*

10. **A GIRL OF SEVENTEEN IS THE ONLY PERSON IN THAT ROOM WHO IS NOT ON THE FRONT OF A PAGE, AND IT IS BECAUSE SHE WAS CAREFUL, AND BEING UNFINDABLE COST HER NOTHING.** Said in four sentences in a room on the fifty-second day of the Long Month, day 207, and about four people in that room wanted to argue and about nine of them did not, and the last thing she said is that being unfindable is a thing she did on purpose and being findable is a thing that was done to somebody else and that neither of those is fair. **Her own slate has her name at the top of it and she read the compact out of a plain slate, and this is the reason and it has not improved and she did not give it again.** *Unresolved on purpose. A chapter may not resolve it and may not make being unfindable a qualification.*

11. **THE NAME AT THE FRONT OF THE SIXTEENTH SHAPE WAS READ ON THE FRONT OF A COPY IN A ROOM ON THE FIFTY-SECOND DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND WAS NOT READ OUT, AND THE SECOND NAME WAS, AND THE READING STOPPED THERE.** Four names are on the front of that page and a woman of about twenty-four read the first line with her hand flat on it and did not read it and read the second name out loud and then stopped. **The name is not printed in this manuscript and `state/batch-summaries/volume-04-batch-0003.md` §8 is still binding and this batch honoured it: it is on a page in the world and it is on no page you can read, it was not printed, it was not supplied, and nobody in any room said it aloud.** *Active as a rule about writing.*

12. **A SACK, A PLACE AT A FIRE, AND A DAY OF THE QUEUE ARE THREE COSTS AND NOT ONE PRICE, AND A MAN OF ABOUT FORTY-FOUR WHO KEEPS A STALL HAS ELEVEN ENTRIES IN A COLUMN OF HIS OWN WITH NO FIGURE ON THE END OF ANY OF THEM.** Said out loud on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month, day 201, at about the ninth hour of the morning, to about nine people who did not ask, and said again in a room on the fifty-second. **A cost is what a person says they are paying and a price is what a person says it is worth, and a price with a figure on the end of it can be wrong in a way a cost in words cannot, and a man of about thirty-nine said in a yard that he would rather have a page with a mistake in it than a wall of good sentences, and then said no on one on the fifty-first, and the column was not recited in these ten chapters.** *Unchanged and not carried forward as a rule about the compact.*

13. **THE DAYS WENT BACK AND THE SACK DID NOT STOP, AND NOBODY IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES HAS WORKED OUT WHETHER A THING GIVEN IN EXCHANGE FOR DAYS THAT HAVE BEEN GIVEN BACK IS A THING THAT STILL COMES.** Said in her own words on the step of a house at the bottom of the Redroot cut on the forty-eighth day of the Long Month, day 203, a Monday, to about four people, and a woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register wrote it down on a second slate and read it back once. **A sack came on the twenty-fifth day of the Long Month, day 180, and one is due, and the woman who keeps that house has not asked and about four other people in that house have not asked, and the reason she gives is that asking is a person saying out loud that she does not trust a man, and about four hundred and forty people on the flats would hear about it inside four days and about nine of them have never been on a rota.** *Open and deliberate. No chapter may ask and nobody may answer it.*

14. **THE KEEPER OF THE REDROOT GATE IS TWENTY-FOUR DAYS INTO A YEAR OF BEING ANGRY ON THE FORTY-EIGHTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND THIRTY DAYS INTO IT ON THE FIFTY-FOURTH, AND THE YEAR IS NOT OVER, AND ON THE DAY SHE NAMED NOBODY WENT.** She chose the date on her own ground on the Sunday of the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month and told nobody. **On the forty-eighth, day 203, a Monday, about nine people walked eleven miles up the road with a reason and a date, and a woman of about fifty-nine who keeps that house and a boy of eight came out onto the step and said the day came, and about nine people stood on a road they had walked eleven miles for and did not go any further, and about four people in that road have said that the date arrived and that nobody went and that nobody is going to.** She worked the channel from about the ninth hour of the morning on the forty-eighth with the board in her left hand and did not put it down once, and she did not say one word to anybody about the forty-eighth, the twenty-fourth, or a year. **She was not rescued, was not argued with, was not given a reason to have done otherwise, has not said she was glad and has not said she would say it again, and the phrase *the whole of her* is not used about her in these ten chapters.** *Open and not to be closed by a date.*

15. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS DERIVED FROM AN ANCHOR AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER.** The Long Month day number is the chapter's own day less 155. The posting went up on day 201 and the days it has been up are the day less 201. The causeway has had nothing on it since the morning of the forty-second day of the Long Month, day 197, and the days it has stood empty are the day less 197: seven at Chapter 0204, thirteen at Chapter 0210. The water stopped coming down the channel on the forty-first day of the Long Month, day 196, and the days it has been stopped are the day less 196: six at Chapter 0202, fourteen at Chapter 0210. The trestle count is the day less forty: one hundred and sixty-five on the fiftieth day of the Long Month, day 205, and the number on the wall in the room over a yard is the figure for the forty-third day of that month and the two differ by seven. The days the thirty-nine men under the near gate have gone unpaid are the day less eighty-five, the first of March being the sixtieth day of the new month: a hundred and nineteen at Chapter 0204 and a hundred and twenty-five at Chapter 0210. The days the thirty-nine days' credit at the store runs for are the fifty-sixth day of the Long Month, day 211, which is the last Tuesday a wage can come out of the chest and which is the day after Chapter 0210. The keeper's year began on the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month, day 179, and her days into it are the Long Month day less twenty-four. **The interval since the seventh week of this flood, day 49, and the interval since the thirty-seventh day of the Long Month, day 192, are written as anchors in this batch and not as figures, and that is the same repair Volume 04's last batch made to the seventh week and it was needed again on the first day.** *The count of this city's silences is one hundred and four at day 200 and one hundred and fourteen at day 210, both derived, and it is on no page in these ten chapters and the wall said nothing in all ten.*

16. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE AND DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0201 TO 0210 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM CHAPTER 0200.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 201 to 210: 201 Saturday, 202 Sunday, 203 Monday, 204 Tuesday, 205 Wednesday, 206 Thursday, 207 Friday, 208 Saturday, 209 Sunday, 210 Monday. **Every weekday word inside the ten that refers to its own day or to a dated day before it was extracted by script and read against the day it names, and the back-references that are on a page are the thirty-sixth day of the Long Month, a Wednesday; the thirty-ninth, a Saturday; the forty-first, a Monday; the forty-third, a Wednesday; the forty-fourth, a Thursday; the forty-fifth, a Friday; the forty-sixth, a Saturday; the forty-ninth, a Tuesday; the fifty-first, a Thursday; the fifty-sixth, a Tuesday; and the twenty-first, a Tuesday. Day 210 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch.**

17. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a page on a wall, a figure in a chest with a date on a slate, a name said out loud once in a room, a refusal nobody can record, a line with no name against it, and a girl of seventeen who cannot be fetched. **Adrian remains Stage 2, Passage holder, in the doing. He performed no working, no threshold was opened, the Narrow Mark was not opened a fourth time and was not looked at in this batch, as in the seven batches before it, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, no chapter offered him a workway and he asked for none, and no chapter turned the name he did not choose into a qualification.**

---

## 28. Rules established in Volume 05, Chapters 0211–0220

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the second ten days of Volume 05, which is the second movement. §23 is not changed, because §23.19 is the close of Volume 03; §24 is not changed, because §24 is the close of the first twenty chapters of Volume 04; §25 is not changed; §26 is not changed, because §26 is the close of Volume 04; and §27 is not changed, because §27 is the close of Volume 05's first ten days. The volume's close record is still `state/volume-04-close.md`, the batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-05-batch-0002.md`, and `outline/volume-05.md` does not exist and this section does not decide what Volume 05 is.**

1. **THE CLOCK WAS ONE DAY AND IT RAN OUT ON THE FIFTY-SIXTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, DAY 211, A TUESDAY, AND A WAGE CAME OUT OF THE SETTLEMENT'S CHEST THAT MORNING TO ABOUT A HUNDRED AND FORTY PEOPLE, AND A MAN OF FORTY-TWO OPENED THAT CHEST IN THE MIDDLE OF A YARD IN DAYLIGHT IN FRONT OF ABOUT NINE PEOPLE BECAUSE HE COULD NOT HOLD IT IN HIS OWN HANDS, AND THERE WAS SALT IN IT.** It is the settlement's own salt off the settlement's own racks. About four of the sacks had been opened and re-tied and the knots on those four were not the knots on the others. He said he did not know that until the lid was off, and he chose a yard over the back room on purpose, and nobody in that yard asked him what was going to happen, and **it is entered that about four of them decided before the lid was off that they were not going to ask him that in a yard. A chapter may not say what a wage in salt is and may not write a plan down, and the ten days of Batch 0002 did not and did not decide one.**

2. **THE THIRTY-NINE MEN UNDER THE NEAR GATE OF THE LOCK OF SALT ARE A HUNDRED AND TWENTY-SIX DAYS UNPAID ON THAT DAY, SAID ONCE IN A YARD, ATTACHED TO THE DECISION THAT NOBODY IN THAT YARD IS GOING TO TELL THEM BECAUSE THERE IS NO TELLING OF THEM THAT IS NOT AN ARGUMENT.** The first of March is the sixtieth day of the new month, which is day 85, and the figure is the day less eighty-five. They are on no line of the posting and have not been since the forty-sixth day of the Long Month. **The figure is paid for once in ten chapters and is not used again in Batch 0002 and a later writer may not use it again without a change.**

3. **THE NINTH LINE IS THE ONLY LINE OF THE POSTING THAT CAN BE CHECKED, AND IT IS THE ONLY LINE OF THE ELEVEN THAT WAS NOT ABOUT ANYBODY'S LABOUR, AND THE MAN OF ABOUT THIRTY-FOUR WHO PUT IT THERE SAYS THAT A PAGE WITH ONE HANDLE ON IT IS NOT A BETTER PAGE THAN A PAGE WITH NONE.** Nine miles of road, a building, and about a hundred and twenty people standing in a line in front of a door is a check anybody in four hundred miles can make. A sack, a place at a fire, a refusal, a road, a piece of ground and a thing somebody said in a kitchen are not. **He said in a yard on the sixty-third day of the Long Month that he would take it off the ninth line and is not going to, and the reason is that the queue's value has never once depended on a page and if the ninth line came off then the only thing ever said about the queue being the only functioning way out of this world would be said in about nine rooms by about four people who remember it. The ninth line has not been moved and may not be moved.**

4. **WHAT THE NINTH LINE IS WORTH IS EXACTLY WHAT THE QUEUE WAS WORTH BEFORE IT WAS WRITTEN DOWN, AND A POSTING IS A PAGE ABOUT LABOUR AND A PAGE ABOUT LABOUR IS NOT A WAGE.** Said in four sentences in a market in daylight on the sixtieth day of the Long Month, with no figure in any of them, and nobody improved on it. **Whether the ninth line WORKS is a different question and it was asked out loud in that market by a man of about thirty-nine who trades on the Open Hand board and was not answered, and it is not answered in Batch 0002 and may not be resolved by a later batch.**

5. **THE PRICES ON A POSTING ARE A SACK, A PLACE AT A FIRE AND A DAY OF THE QUEUE, AND TWO OF THE THREE ARE A PERSON SHOWING UP SOMEWHERE.** Said by a man of about fifty-two who digs in a yard on the fifty-eighth day of the Long Month, and he said the thing about a place at a fire first and said he was not saying it was a bad thing. **A place at a fire is a thing a person can be found at, because somebody has to see a person to give them one, and a day of the queue is a thing a person can be found in, for the same reason, and about nine people in this city had been carrying a sack past a fire for about nine weeks without noticing it. Nobody has ever put that on a page about anything and this is the first time it has been said out loud in daylight and it is on a page now.**

6. **THE STORE'S OWN BOARD IS WASHED EVERY MORNING, IT CARRIES THE FIGURES OF THE DAY BEFORE, IT IS WRITTEN AT THE SEVENTH HOUR WHEN THE MAN WHO WASHES IT KNOWS WHAT THE DAY WAS, AND IT IS ABOUT A FOOT TO THE LEFT OF THE POSTING ON THE SAME WALL.** About nine people in that yard read the board and about four read the posting, and no chapter in Batch 0002 moved either object, and **nobody had said out loud that the two things were a foot apart until a man of about twenty-seven who washes it said so on the fifty-sixth day of the Long Month.** A figure on that wall is a day old on the morning after the day it is about. *A board with a day on it is correct about that day and about nothing else, and a posting is a page with a day at the top of it and no figure under any of its prices, and the man who washes the board cannot say which of the two he would rather have written.*

7. **A SACK AND A NINTH OF A SACK WENT ON A BEAM AND TWO PANES ON THE FIFTY-EIGHTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH, AND THE NINTH IS THE ONLY FIGURE THAT HAS EVER EXISTED UNDER ANY OF THE THREE PRICES, AND IT IS ON A SLATE IN A WOMAN'S HAND AND NOT ON THE WALL.** The beam and the pans were made by a man of about fifty-two who digs out of a length of iron and two lengths of wood, and he said the ninth and not the number so that whoever wrote it down would have to put a thing in their own hand on it. **A figure under one line of three says the other two are worth nothing, and the man of about thirty-four who wrote the page is not entitled to say that about a fire and a queue, and he said so in a yard about nine minutes after he had refused to say it to the person who asked him.** A later writer may not put the figure on the wall and may not have him put it there himself.

8. **THE FIGURE CAME OFF IRON FOUND AT THE BOTTOM OF A CUT IN THE SEVENTH WEEK OF THIS FLOOD AND WOOD OFF A RACK, AND THE MAN WHO MADE THE BEAM CANNOT SAY WHOSE EITHER, AND HE HAS HAD A THING ON IT EVERY DAY SINCE THE FORTY-SECOND DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AND HAD NOT THOUGHT ABOUT THAT ONCE.** He says that a thing with no name on it is the only kind of thing there is. *Open. A chapter may not find out whose gate the iron came off.*

9. **A HOLE THAT FOUR PEOPLE MEASURE IS A COLUMN ABOUT A HOLE, AND A MAN OF ABOUT FIFTY-TWO WHO DIGS PUT A ROD IN A DIFFERENT HOLE ON THE FIFTY-NINTH DAY OF THE LONG MONTH AFTER SAYING ON THE FIFTY-FIFTH THAT HE HAD STOPPED MEASURING.** The reason he gave out loud is that a sack went onto a beam nine miles up the road on the fifty-eighth day of that month and he cannot find out from a rod whether the salt in it came out of that water. **He is going to measure a hole nobody has measured and he is not going to tell anybody the answer, because he has not got one and because about nine people will write a number down instead of it.** The second hole is nine feet from the measured one. *Open, and the four weeks is still a direction and not a number and he cannot say what day the water comes back.*

10. **A RACK IS FOUR LENGTHS OF WOOD AND A ROPE AND FOUR PEOPLE, ABOUT NINE OF THE RACKS CAN BE WORKED AND EVERY ONE OF THOSE NINE IS ON SOMEBODY'S ROTA, AND THE SETTLEMENT'S NINE ARE THE ONLY NINE PEOPLE IN FOUR HUNDRED MILES WHO HAVE BEEN ASKED WHETHER THEY WANT A DAY.** Said by a man who has carried racks up and down a cut since the second month of this flood and has never once been asked to work one and is not asking. **About four hundred and forty people are on eleven miles of flats and about nine of them are on a rota and the rest have never been on one.** *Open. Volume 05 may not write a line for them and may not reach the buyer nine hundred miles away.*
