   **AND THREE ROUND FIGURES FALL INSIDE VOLUME 07 AND ALL THREE ARE TRAPS, AND ALL THREE ARE NAMED HERE SO THAT NO BATCH WALKS INTO ONE: the posting is exactly a hundred days old at day 301; the trestle count is exactly three hundred at day 340; and the bag on the boards is exactly a hundred days old at day 340.** A figure that comes out round has been checked twice and not once.

6. **AND THE COUNT OF SILENCES, WHICH IS THE ONE DERIVED FIGURE IN THIS SECTION THAT CANNOT BE GOT BY ADDING, AND IT IS PUBLISHED HERE BECAUSE A LATER WRITER WILL ADD IT WRONG.** The rule is one addition for every day the wall says nothing, and **the wall said something on day 256, which spent Volume 06's one panel, and that day is therefore not a silence.** One hundred and fifty-two is on a page at `chapter-0250.md` and is the figure at day 250. **Two hundred and one at day 300 is that base plus fifty less one and it is the figure Volume 06 published. Two hundred and two at day 301 is the same base plus fifty-one less one. Two hundred and fifty at day 350 is the same base plus one hundred less two, because Volume 07 spends its one panel on day 340 and that is the second day this flood on which the wall said something. ONE HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO PLUS ONE HUNDRED IS TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY-TWO, LESS TWO IS TWO HUNDRED AND FIFTY, AND THE FIGURE PRINTED HERE BEFORE THIS AMENDMENT WAS TWO HUNDRED AND FORTY-NINE, WHICH WAS THAT same expression off by one and is withdrawn.** The figure is for the state layer and is not for a chapter, and no chapter of Volume 07 prints it.

7. **AND THE SIX NOTICES OF VOLUME 07, BECAUSE THE FOUR DAYS OF NOTICE IS AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT A COURTESY AND A CHECK OF NUMERALS CANNOT SEE IT.** A notice on any day other than the fourth day before the room is a three-day instrument, and a three-day instrument does not fail, it arrives warm, and a batch that has written one has produced a chapter with every figure in it correct and an instrument in it that does not exist. Four batches in a row in Volume 06 paid for that. **All six of Volume 07's notices are here, and every one of them is on the page in the chapter the outline names, with the morning named inside that chapter:** the household on the lane, room on day 305, notice on day 301, which is Chapter 0301 and is the volume's first page; the first of the four, room on day 330 a Tuesday, notice on **day 326** in Chapter 0326; the second, room on day 331, notice on **day 327** in Chapter 0327; the third, room on day 332, notice on **day 328** in Chapter 0328; the fourth, room on day 333, notice on **day 329** in Chapter 0329; **and the man who digs, room on day 339 a Thursday, notice on day 335 in Chapter 0335.** Six notices, six chapters, four days each, unsigned, with no name at the top of any of them. **A notice goes to a place and not to a house.**

8. **AND THE STANDING RULE CARRIED FORWARD UNCHANGED, AND IT IS THE LARGEST CLASS OF FAULT IN THIS MANUSCRIPT.** Four interval faults in Volume 06 Batch 0001, six in 0002, four in 0003, six in 0004 and eleven in 0005, against twenty-one, seventeen, eleven, nineteen, seven and eleven in Volume 05's five batches and its repair pass. **An outline is where those counts are born. A value at a chapter boundary is derived; a derived value may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to another chapter or to a mouth. Where two elapsed counts sit in one sentence, the event each is measured from has to be named in the sentence. And where a figure has two plausible anchors, name both anchors in the same sentence.**

## 34. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0251–0260 — WHAT THE FIRST TEN DAYS OF THE VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the first ten days of Volume 06. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, and in particular §33.1's amendment of the fourth form's range, §33.3's registration of the fifth form, §33.4's five forms and §33.5's weekday anchors all stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 is the house formatting rule and is not changed. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0251 to 0260 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md` and the volume's close record for Volume 05 is `state/volume-05-close.md`, which is not superseded by this section.**

1. **A CROWN INSTRUMENT ARRIVED, AND IT IS CORRECT, AND IT HAS DONE NOTHING.** A case the size of a bread chest came off a barge at the salt wharf on the ninety-sixth day of the Long Month, went up nine miles on a handcart, and has been shut on a table in a room over a market since. The docket is tied twice to the handle in oiled cloth under a seal the colour of a wet stone, and the docket has a date at the top in a form nobody in this city writes, four lines, a clerk's name at the foot in a hand nobody in the room has, and a place at the bottom nine hundred miles away. **The four lines, read out in the local language in daylight with a date on the door: an emergency is declared in the crossing and in the ground adjoining the crossing; the days upon every gate between this city and the gates of the west road are to be entered in a schedule; the schedule is to be made up and kept by a company; and in every line of that schedule there are four places, and the four places are a date, a gate, a holder, and the days.** *Open. A later writer may not open the case, may not fill the schedule, may not convert the instrument into a threat with a face in it, and may not improve on the four lines.*

2. **IT DOES NOT NAME THE STORE, AND THAT IS THE ONLY PROTECTION ANYBODY IN THIS CITY HAS, AND IT WORKS BY ACCIDENT.** The instrument names no building, no yard, no door and no queue. **A document that does not name a thing is not a thing that takes it.** The store is on a page in one place only, in the ninth line of a posting written on purpose by a man of about thirty-four on the forty-sixth day of the Long Month, and he has not moved it and has refused five times to say whether it works. *Open and not to be improved on.*

3. **THE WORD *HOLDER* IS ON A PAGE AND IS STILL NOT DEFINED BY ANYBODY.** It is on a page in four mouths across Chapters 0251 to 0254 and about nine people in this city did not know whether it was a word for a thing or a word for a person. *Open. A later writer may not define it without a room, in daylight, with a date on it.*

4. **REGENT SOLENNE DARY IS A PERSON ON A PAGE, AND WHAT SHE DID NOT SAY IS THE SENTENCE THE VOLUME TURNS ON.** Said out loud in the local language, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door, by **the man of forty-one in a very good coat**, who has been in the room at the top of that road. Her nine sentences in his mouth, and not in hers: a quarterly return has been in her hand four times a year; the return has a district on it; the district has a population on it; the district has no rota on it; the district has no share on it; there is no name on any roll she can find for that district and she has had clerks looking for four quarters; a road kept by a body that answers to nothing and cannot be counted is not a road, it is a rumour; in a bad season a rumour kills people before it has been found out to have been one. **She did not say the days on a gate are the Crown's to schedule, because nobody asked her, and the asking is the thing, and it is the one thing a state nine hundred miles away cannot do to itself.** *Open. A later writer may not put it in her mouth without a room, in daylight, with a date on it, and may not name the figure on her return, which is on a page in a building nine hundred miles from this one and is not on a page here.*

5. **THE FIFTH FORM IS ON A PAGE, AND THE NAME, THE REASON, THE REFUSAL AND THE REASON FOR THE REFUSAL ARE IN CHAPTER 0256 AND NOWHERE ELSE.** *The Nth day of the Short Month*, the Nth being day 255 + N, from day 256, sixty days long, ending on day 315. Named by **Sera Vail, a notary of fifty, twenty-six years a notary**, in a room in daylight with a date in it, **after a woman of about seventy from a village of nine households that has refused four times had refused and given a reason that is hers, and with both the refusal and the reason entered in her words.** **Day 256 is the hundred and first day of the Long Month by the fourth form and the first day of the Short Month by the fifth, and both are on a page on the same table, and both are correct, and only one of them was chosen by a person.** *Open as to use and closed as to naming. §33.3 stands and this item records where the name went.*

6. **THE VOLUME'S ONE PANEL, AND WHAT IT COST.** Plain, one line, in its own block, no emphasis marks, and the only instrument-shaped thing in Chapters 0251 to 0260: ***A name given to a thing so that the thing can go in a column is not the name of the thing.*** It is refused out loud in about four seconds by the notary of fifty, and what it costs is that she has just invented a name in a room and she is going to keep the name. The refusal is entered as a refusal out loud and the count of refusals said out loud in this city moves by one, and the two counts in two books in this city that have never been made smaller are not made smaller by it. **The wall said nothing on the other nine days of this batch.** *Open. It is a rule and not an answer, and a later writer may not repeat it, may not build on it, and may not spend the volume's resolution on it.*

7. **A HOLDING COMPANY IS ENTERED BY THE NAME OF THE PLACE IT KEEPS, AND NO WORD WAS INVENTED.** The place is **Hallowgate**, a gate and not an office and not a district, and there is a keeper at that gate and **the keeper has not been asked anything by anybody.** The people from the other side are on that roll **one line each, by the name each said out loud in a room in daylight with a date on it, with no class on it**, as named guests under **the compact read out on day 154**, whose fifth line is that a guest may ask to stop being a guest, and the asking is the whole of it and no reason is asked for and it is not asked twice. **It is that compact and not the other one, and the reason is entered in the room in Chapter 0258, and the reason is that the other compact's ninth line is a person who may be found and cannot be ended by asking at all.** *Open. **A guest is the only instrument in four hundred miles that a person can end by asking, and a company of guests is not, and that is the finding.** A later writer may not invent a word, may not add a class to a line, and may not amend the posting.*

8. **A COMPANY ENTERED BY A PLACE CANNOT BE POSTED A WAGE, AND A WAGE THAT CANNOT BE POSTED IS A FAVOUR, AND A FAVOUR IS A THING THAT STOPS.** Said in a mouth in a yard on the third day of the Short Month, and about nine people will be paid out of a pocket this week, and the chapter says that in a mouth and not in narration. *Open. The favour stops in Chapter 0277 and about nine people stop turning up, and a later writer may not repair it by inventing a word.*

9. **A HETHEGUARD'S REFUSAL BINDS A BODY AND IS NOT AN OFFICE, A POST, A SEAT, A WAGE OR A COLUMN, AND NOBODY CAN BE WRITTEN TO FOR HAVING BEEN REFUSED BY HIM.** Said on the fourth day of the Short Month before the sentence was finished, on the grounds — nine words, and the whole of it — that **nobody here carries a Crown case but the Hearthguard**, and then the one thing in that room that is his and is not a refusal: **a written asking, dated four days ahead, that the case be read to the three villages by somebody who is not carrying it, which nobody in four hundred miles has ever asked for.** *Open. **A refusal out loud has to cost somebody something specific and this one cost a season's worth of being told nothing, and no chapter may convert a season into a number of days, and how many people are in those three villages is a figure on no page and this volume does not supply one.***

10. **THE QUESTION IS IN A HAND THAT IS NOT ADRIAN VALE'S, AND IT IS NOT SIGNED, AND IT IS NOT PRINTED.** Four conditions in his hand and handed over — the question in the person's own words, in writing, to everybody who will be in the room, four days before — and a question written on a piece of paper with nothing on the top of it, in her own hand, and not signed, and the reason is hers and it is nine years old. **The question itself is on no page in Chapters 0251 to 0260 and a later writer may not print it until a chapter that is not a batch's to decide.** *Open.*

11. **A NAME GIVEN OUT LOUD IN A MARKET BY A PERSON WHO COULD NOT CHECK IT IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS NOT A COUNT.** **Ninety-four gates**, said out loud on the hundredth day of the Long Month, and nobody in that market could check it, and about four people wrote it down, and the woman who said it said in the same breath that nobody in four hundred miles has ever counted them and that she had it out of about nine people. **This is the first number this volume has put on a page that nobody can check, and it is the volume's subject and not a property of the road. A later writer may not treat it as a count, may not correct it, may not improve on it, and may not build anything on it, and it is not the four hundred and forty on eleven miles of flats and must never be read as a variant of it.**

12. **AND THE THINGS THE FIRST TEN DAYS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved, and the register-keeper in Chapter 0258 read the eight above the ninth in order off a slate the man of about thirty-nine copied off the wall that morning, and stopped, and said what the ninth is in her own words without reading it. **The wall itself is nine miles up the road and is in no room, and the posting's order on the wall is the compact's first seven, then the compact's eleventh, then the store and its queue written last at the bottom as the ninth.** The board was not written on and the wall above it was not washed. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, and the pan was not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two brooms are on their boards. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is in none of these ten chapters. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not the string *Redroot*, which was at zero and proved nothing, but a physical one: the Marches' gatekeeper in Chapter 0260 was given the keeper's whole working silhouette and has been taken apart from her in verb, tool, material, room and light, and the two share none of it.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale. The about four hundred and forty are not in these ten chapters. The girl of seventeen is on a page in two of them and is on no roll and is unresolved. The delegation has not left. *Open, all of it, and the whole of it is at `state/volume-05-close.md` §3 and §4 and is not restated at less than its length here.*

13. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a case on a table, four places on a page, a word nobody can define, a name, a refusal, a second reading, a form, a month, a company that cannot be posted, a number of gates nobody has counted, and a question in a hand that is not his. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate**, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named. *Open.*

14. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED.** The Long Month day is the day less 155 to day 255 and the Short Month day is the day less 255 from day 256. The posting went up on day 201, so its age is the day less 201 and it is on a page at fifty on day 251 and fifty-two on day 253. The board's figures are for day 224, so their age is the day less 224, and **nine of the ten values are on a page, twenty-eight to thirty-six, and Chapter 0251 names the figures' own day and the day after them as the last day it was written on and states no age.** The board has not been written on since day 225 and its unwritten age is the day less 225 and one value of that is on a page, twenty-seven on day 252. The wind has been at that wall since day 229 and its age is the day less 229 and **every one of the ten values is on a page, twenty-two to thirty-one.** The trestle count is the day less forty and is **two hundred and thirteen on a page on the two hundred and fifty-third day of this flood, in narration, dated, with the fortieth day named in the same sentence, and no other value of it is on a page in these ten chapters.** The case came up the river on day 251, so its age on a table is the day less 251 and **eight is on a page on day 259.** A day of this flood is inclusive: the two hundred and fifty-first day is day 251 and the two hundred and fifty-second is day 252, and the inclusive figures on a page in these ten chapters are 251, 252, 253, 255, 256, 258 and 259, and all seven are correct. The nine measures of hard salt under a shop counter have been there since the thirty-ninth day of the Long Month and their age is not on a page and is not stated as a count. The keeper's year began on day 179 and **no chapter of this batch counts it, names it, or uses her, and the figure is published in the batch record and in the state layer and not in a chapter.** **A later writer may not carry any chain from a neighbouring chapter or from a state file, and the value at a chapter boundary is derived and may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to a mouth unless a page carries it.**

15. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0251 TO 0260 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM CHAPTER 0250 AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 251 to 260: 251 Sunday, 252 Monday, 253 Tuesday, 254 Wednesday, 255 Thursday, 256 Friday, 257 Saturday, 258 Sunday, 259 Monday, 260 Tuesday. **Day 250 is a Saturday by that arithmetic and is on no page in any form — `chapters/volume-05/chapter-0250.md` gives no weekday for its own day — and therefore day 251 is a Sunday.** The map was validated by recomputing days 231 to 250 out of the same two founding figures and reproducing the run published in `state/batch-summaries/volume-05-batch-0004.md` §3 before it was used on any of this batch's own days. **Day 255 carries one chapter and day 256 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and no double day in this volume.**

16. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE, BECAUSE THE FIRST TEN DAYS OF THIS VOLUME WERE WRITTEN AGAINST A MEASURED FINDING AND THE MEASUREMENT IS ON THE PAGE.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250, 175 of 176 plain speeches were answered by a bolded speech in the next line, and across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629. **Across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, and the longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs in any of the ten is four, which is two exchanges, and the rule that governs it is in `outline/volume-06.md` §17.6 and is not a rule a later batch may relax.** The bolded share of words in the ten runs 10.4 to 21.7 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and a ceiling of about 35. **A bolded turn has a mean of forty words and a maximum of ninety-two, and the maximum is published because the first version of this section printed the mean's ceiling as if it were the maximum, and three turns in the batch are at or past `outline/volume-06.md` §17.4's eighty words and stand, with the reasons at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md` §11.6.** **And a sentence of forty words or more may not stand verbatim in more than one chapter of a batch, which is a check that costs one script and which this batch failed sixty-seven times before its review repair pass fixed it.** A number is not a substitute for the reading and these are printed so that a reviewer can check them.

---

## 35. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0261–0270 — WHAT THE SECOND TEN DAYS OF THE VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the second ten days of Volume 06. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED BY THIS SECTION, and every one of its figures, its fifth form and its weekday anchors stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 is the house formatting rule and is not changed. §34 is the first ten days of this volume and is not changed and not re-stated at less than its length here. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0261 to 0270 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` and the record of the first ten days is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md`.**

1. **THE SECOND OF THE FOUR PLACES HAS NOWHERE TO START, AND THAT IS THE BATCH'S FINDING AND NOT A DECISION.** A Crown schedule wants the days upon every gate between this city and the gates of the west road entered in a schedule of four places, **and a room over a market found out on the two hundred and sixty-first day of this flood that it could not write even the first line of one because it did not know where the first gate was.** Six gates on that road were walked and gave six answers, **two of which were about a turn and not one of which was a name, and the only list anybody in this city now has of anything to do with that road is four knots in a piece of string that a boy of thirteen tied on a cart rail and that nobody asked for.** The first of the four places is a date and the fourth of the four is still empty and the third has a candidate this city will not name (§4 below). *Open. A later writer may not resolve which gate is which, may not print the knots, and may not turn a turn into a rota.*

2. **A PAGE OF THIS CROWN'S INSTRUMENT HAS BEEN READ OUT IN FOUR KITCHENS IN THREE VILLAGES BY ACCIDENT, AND NOBODY SENT IT.** On the thirteenth day of the Short Month the rain got under a tarpaulin on a cart going nine days up the coast road to a house, **and a woman who is the account of documents read the four places out in four kitchens between the second hour of the afternoon and about the sixth, and a carter of about thirty-five did not know what was under his own sacking and had not known for about a month.** The sheet is the fifth of the five and it is the one with the four places ruled on it and nothing written in them, and **it is now on a page in three villages and nobody in four hundred miles knows which of the four kitchens read it the same way as the others.** *Open. A later writer may not fill in any of the four places, may not say whether the four readings agreed, and may not make the reading into a delivery.*

3. **A GUEST ASKED TO STOP BEING A GUEST, IN NINE WORDS, AND NO REASON WAS ASKED FOR AND IT WAS NOT ASKED TWICE, AND THE ROLL AFTERWARDS HAS EIGHT GUESTS AND ONE PERSON ON IT AND THERE IS NO LINE FOR THE PERSON.** Said out loud in a room, in daylight, with a date on the door, in a market town out past the last named house, under **the compact read out on day 154 whose fifth line is the guest line**, and **no reason was asked for, and the asking is the whole of it, and it is not asked twice.** The heading at the top of the roll is the name of a gate and **there is no class on any of the nine lines and no word anywhere above the names except the name of a place.** *Open. A later writer may not add a line for the person who asked, may not invent a word for what he is now, and may not resolve whether the roll of eight is a company that can be posted a wage — **it cannot, and that is on a page.***

4. **A WAGE IS AN AMOUNT, A DAY TO PAY ON AND A NAME TO PAY, AND THE PAGE HAS ONLY THE NAME.** Said in a mouth in that market town on the twelfth day of the Short Month, in three pieces, **and not one of the three is that the page is bad.** A company entered by the name of the place it keeps therefore cannot be posted a wage, **and a page with a name and no amount and no day is not a page a wage can be posted on, and the Crown's schedule is the first thing in about nine months that has wanted to give anybody on that road a day to be paid on, and about four offices that do not exist stand between the page and the person.** *Open. **The favour stops in Chapter 0277 and about nine people stop turning up, and a later writer may not repair it by inventing a word, and this section does not restate §34.8 at less than its length.***

5. **THE THIRD PLACE HAS A CANDIDATE AND THE ROOM'S REFUSAL TO NAME HIM IS NOT FREE.** A man of about fifty-five keeps the first gate of that road, **and he keeps it because he opens it, and nobody has handed him a better reason and he has never asked for one, and he has a chain on it because a cart came through in the night in the first month of this flood and he has not slept easier about it, and nobody has ever asked him anything at all in a hundred days, and he said *do not come back with it in a book*.** A page with a person's name in it is a description and a description is a removal with a column for where the person is, **and the second of the four places is unstartable, and the third of the four has exactly one candidate in four hundred miles, and this city will not put him in it and nobody has been asked, and about four people in this city have worked out that a refusal to name him is a refusal that leaves a page nine hundred miles away free to send somebody who will.** *Open. A later writer may not name him, may not ask him, may not put his name in a column, and may not have the Crown name him in a chapter that is not Chapter 0276 or later and not in a mouth that is a clerk's.*

6. **A HOUSEHOLD'S DAYS ARE NOT A SCHEDULE, AND THIS IS A HOUSEHOLD'S OWN FINDING AND IT IS NOT THE SENTENCE FROM CHAPTER 0154.** Said out loud in a kitchen with no paper in it on the eleventh day of the Short Month, in a woman's mouth, and **she refused to say what her days are, and the reason is hers and it is nine years old.** A schedule is a set of days with a thing held against them and every day in it is the same as every other day in it; **a household's days are a set of things that happen in a set order and the order moves with the weather and with how many hands there are and with whose child is awake, and there is no way a person could stand at the end of a week and put a number against Monday and have it be right on Monday.** *Open, and **the household is the finding of day 294 and a later writer may not bring it forward, and a chapter may not put the sentence from Chapter 0154 into this woman's mouth and may not say that her sentence is that sentence said differently.***

7. **THE WORD *HOLDER* IS ON A PAGE IN THIS CITY AND NOW ON A PAGE IN THE MARCHES, AND IT IS STILL NOT DEFINED BY ANYBODY.** Asked for out loud in a kitchen out past the last named house by a woman with flour to the wrist, in front of about four children, **and the answer given was that this village has the thing the third place is asking for, and that the thing is that a bar goes round, and that writing it down makes a name on a page, and that a name on a page is a place where a person used to be.** *Open. A later writer may not define it without a room, in daylight, with a date on it, and may not have the same woman give two versions.*

8. **THE CONSENT FRACTURE IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS UNPAID.** **Adrian Vale used what Tamsin Quill knows about the second way on the fifth sheet without saying what the working would cost her, and she caught it in one sentence, and she said that a thing you know and do not say is a thing you have chosen, and that she wanted him to want it, and that he did not do it on purpose and that that is worse.** The second way is a reading entered in a register under the name of a person who can be shown to have made it, **and she is the only person in that village who can read a clerk's hand and is not a household, and a name on a register that can be looked up is a name in a column.** *Open, and **it is paid in Chapter 0272 and a later writer may not pay it earlier and may not resolve it here, and the two of them are not reconciled and nobody apologises in Chapter 0270.***

9. **THE WORD *SAFE* IS IN THREE VILLAGES AND THE TWO WORDS A ROOM IN THIS CITY LEFT ON TWO LINES HAVE BEEN JOINED BY ABOUT NINE MILES OF ROAD.** *Safe* and *villages* are on two lines of a wall in a room over a yard in this city and were deliberately not joined. **The word came off a cart of kindling into the second village on the fourteenth day of the Short Month, and by the time the man who had said it got to the second gate the two words were side by side in about nine mouths, and about four people have said since that a word on a page with nothing attached to it is not a word that waits for somebody to attach it.** *Open, and **a later writer may not join those two words in this city, may not say who said the word first in a room, and may not convert eleven weeks into a number of days.***

10. **A ROAD ON THAT STRETCH HAS BEEN KEPT FOR ABOUT A HUNDRED AND FORTY YEARS BY A THIRD THING, AND THE CROWN HAS NOW WRITTEN DOWN TWO WAYS OF KEEPING IT.** A bar bent about a finger's width, a post out at the bottom in the water, and a person who is not the last one to have held it, **and the man who bent it has not had the turn for about nine years, nobody has ever asked him, he is not on a page, and there is no page, and the post has been in the ground about a hundred and forty years.** A rack stands in a channel, a channel takes its water from a gate, and **a rack that has not been worked does not come off** — used as a working fact and not improved on, in three of these ten chapters, by a man quoting a person about a hundred and forty years dead. *Open, and §26.4's mechanism is on the page at Chapter 0195 and again here and may not be reinvented or improved on, and **a later writer may not put the two rolls of gates side by side, because that is Chapter 0280's and it is Volume 07's answer.***

11. **ONE THING WAS ENTERED ON PURPOSE AND IT HAS NO NAME ON IT.** The woman of about twenty-nine who keeps a public register wrote one line on her second slate on the seventh day of the Short Month: the day, the question, and that the room did not answer it. **Five days before that she had refused to put a date on a reading that came from nine hundred miles away, and on the seventh she put one on this and said in a mouth that she had decided it five days earlier and named what it bought: a question with a day on it and no name on it can only be answered by somebody who was in the room.** *Open. **This is the only decision any person in Chapters 0261 to 0270 makes, and a later writer may not enter a name beside that line and may not enter a figure and may not make the room answer the question.***

12. **AND THE THINGS THE SECOND TEN DAYS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line of the posting was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved, and the man who wrote it said nothing at all in a room on the seventh day of the Short Month with a piece of chalk on the table in front of him, and the man who washes the board was in none of these ten chapters and the board was not written on. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, and the pan was not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is in none of these ten chapters. The two brooms are on their boards. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not the string *Redroot*, which is at zero and proves nothing, and not the strings *invert*, *broom*, *Alys*, *Kern* and *silence*, which are at zero and prove less. The proof is a person-by-person read of every woman's physical signature in the ten chapters, and it is at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §6.2.1, and no woman in these ten chapters has a stick, a broom, a board on boards, an invert, or a day of silence.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale. The about four hundred and forty are not in these ten chapters and their chapter is Chapter 0279. The girl of seventeen is in none of these ten chapters. The delegation has not left. *Open, all of it, and the whole of it is at `state/volume-05-close.md` §3 and §4 and is not restated at less than its length here.*

13. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a list of four knots, a bar of oak with an iron shoe, a post out at the bottom, a bent bar, a dry channel, a pail line nobody ordered, a rack that does not come off, nine names on a wall with a gate's name at the top of it, a word attached to nobody, a sheet read out in four kitchens by accident, and a woman who found out what a page cost. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate**, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named. *Open.*

14. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED.** The Short Month day is the day less 255. The store's board carries the figures of day 224, so their age is the day less 224: **thirty-seven at day 261 and forty-six at day 270, and the board has stood unwritten thirty-six to forty-five, and the wind has been at that wall since day 229 and is thirty-two to forty-one.** The case came up the river on day 251, so its age is the day less 251: **ten at day 261 and nineteen at day 270, and eight is on a page at Chapter 0259 and these ten are derived.** **All forty of those values are in narration with the day they are measured from named in the same sentence, and none of them is in a mouth, and none of the ten leads uses a figure that is already on a page. THE VALUES ARE IN THE TEN LEADS AND THE REPAIR PASS AT §36 HAS LEFT THEM THERE AND HAS RECORDED THE DEBT RATHER THAN PAID IT: §17.2 pays a standing thread for once, in a mouth, attached to a decision, and again only when it changes, and none of these four has changed in ten days. The next ten days should carry the board, the wind and the case in mouths and not in leads.** The posting's age, the trestle count, the unpaid figure, the share with the post taken out, the bag, the delegation's age and the keeper's days are **at zero across the ten chapters.** The count of this city's silences is **one hundred and sixty-two at day 261 and one hundred and seventy-one at day 270**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and not in any of the ten chapters. Three figures in these chapters are private and are **not** interval chains and may not be measured off an anchor: a repair at a channel quoted as about nine days of hands and one post, a man's arm at nine days, and the method of saying a name out loud in a room at nine days old. **A later writer may not carry any chain from a neighbouring chapter or from a state file, and the value at a chapter boundary is derived and may be used in a chapter that dates it and may not be attributed to a mouth unless a page carries it.**

15. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0261 TO 0270 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM CHAPTER 0260 AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE AND NOT FROM THE BATCH PROMPT'S TABLE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 261 to 270: 261 Wednesday, 262 Thursday, 263 Friday, 264 Saturday, 265 Sunday, 266 Monday, 267 Tuesday, 268 Wednesday, 269 Thursday, 270 Friday, and the Short Month days six to fifteen. **Day 260 is a Tuesday by that arithmetic and is on no page in any form, and therefore day 261 is a Wednesday.** Forty-one interior weekday tokens were extracted with their contexts and read against the day each names, **and all forty-one are a recurring day that belongs to no date or a day checked against the founding figure, and six interval faults were found in the writing pass and repaired, and the last of the six was a count measured off a day it was not measured from, in a sentence that read perfectly. TWO MORE WERE FOUND AFTERWARDS BY A REVIEWER AND ARE REPAIRED AT §36: Chapters 0261 and 0263 gave the case's age as the right number measured from the first day of the Short Month, which is day 256, and the case came up the river on day 251.** **Day 270 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and no double day in this volume.**

16. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE, BECAUSE THESE TEN DAYS WERE WRITTEN AGAINST A MEASURED FINDING AND THE MEASUREMENT IS ON THE PAGE.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250 it is 175 of 176 plain speeches answered in the next line in bold, and across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629, and across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, **and across Chapters 0261 to 0270 it is 49 of 124, forty per cent, and that is the number a later batch has to beat. THE FIFTEEN PER CENT THIS SECTION CARRIED BEFORE THE REPAIR AT §36 WAS A MEASURE OF THE MARKUP AND NOT OF THE PROSE, because two of the four turns in the volume's own two-hander climax carried no mark at all and three narrative paragraphs carried a mark §17.3 forbids; all four are repaired and the number went up twenty-five points, and a batch that scores well on this metric by leaving half its turns unmarked has not been measured.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs is **four in seven of the ten chapters, three in two and two in one.** The bolded share of words is 9.5 to 21.6 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, **and a bolded turn in the ten has a mean of forty-six words and a maximum of seventy-eight, and the maximum is published because the last volume printed a mean's ceiling as if it were the maximum and a tail grew unreported for a batch, and because the eighty-three this section carried was a breach of §17.4 that was published and defended rather than cut. **A metric that counts only marked turns cannot see a hundred-and-twenty-one-word speech carrying no mark at all, and one of those was in this batch, and a later batch must count the unmarked ones.**** The counting device is 0.55 to 1.10 per hundred words, mean 0.86, and **a rate is not canon and a batch record's own rate is not a target.** **There is no panel in Chapters 0261 to 0270, and there should not be: the volume's one panel was spent on day 256 and `outline/volume-06.md` §17.7 fixes it there, and a day the wall says something on is not a silence.** **And a sentence of forty words or more may not stand verbatim in more than one chapter of a batch: there are 269 long sentences across the ten files of this batch and zero duplicated, and the fall from 281 is the twenty-eight-word lead frame that was carried ten times and has been rewritten, and that check is one script, and a volume built out of recurring interstitial beats is the volume in which it matters, and the beats were all kept and the sentences carrying them were made different in every chapter.** A number is not a substitute for the reading and these are printed so that a reviewer can check them.

## 36. Rules established by the review repair pass on Volume 06, Chapters 0261–0270 — AND THE TWO THAT ARE GENERAL

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §35 IS THE SECOND TEN DAYS OF THIS VOLUME AND IS NOT CHANGED except in five figures that §36.3 names, and every one of those five was wrong. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The full pass is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §14 and it is the record, and this section is the part a later writer has to carry.**

1. **A RULE SATISFIED MECHANICALLY CAN PRODUCE THE THING THE RULE EXISTS TO PREVENT, AND IT DID, IN THIS VOLUME, IN THE FIRST TEN DAYS OF THE BATCH.** The ten leads of Chapters 0261 to 0270 were required to carry four derived interval figures with the day each is measured from named in the same sentence. They carried all forty values and all forty were right. **They also came out as one paragraph written ten times with the numerals changed, which is the recapping ledger that `outline/volume-06.md` §17.2 forbids in any shape, and a reader stops on it, and no check in the batch record could see it, because every check the record ran was a check on the figures.** The general rule this establishes for every batch after this one: **a mechanical check that verifies a required element is not evidence that the required element has been written rather than satisfied. A chapter lead is read by a person and not by a script, and a lead that carries four figures has to be four different sentences.** All ten have been rewritten, no two share a sentence, the forty values are all still on the page, and the anchors are named three ways: *the sixty-ninth day of the Long Month* for the store's board, *the ninety-sixth day of the Long Month* for the case, and *the two hundred and twenty-ninth day of this flood* for the wind at that wall. **That last one is a new anchor in this volume and a later writer may use it, and it is a reference to a day and not a date in the fourth form.**

2. **A COUNT IS CHECKED AGAINST ITS ANCHOR AND NOT AGAINST ITS ARITHMETIC, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME IN THIS VOLUME THAT THE DIFFERENCE MATTERED.** The case's age was ten in Chapter 0261 and twelve in Chapter 0263. Both are right. Both were measured from the first day of the Short Month, which is day 256, and the case came up the river on day 251. **A check that counts forty figures and finds forty numerals has not checked anything an anchor is for.** The same fault was in the two chapters that dated Hettis Roke's refusal, and the same fault was then written into `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §8.4 as a reference that was *not zero and not a fault*, which is the worst of the three available outcomes, because a state file that certifies a slip launders it. **The general rule: an interior date reference in a chapter is re-derived from the chapter that put the day on a page, and never from a state file and never from a sibling chapter's memory of it, and a state file that publishes a non-zero string as approved must name the page it was checked against.**

3. **THE FIVE FIGURES §35 CARRIED THAT WERE WRONG, so that no later writer inherits them.** The case's anchor in Chapters 0261 and 0263; the date of Roke's refusal in Chapters 0262 and 0268 and in §8.4 of the batch record; the fifteen-per-cent exchange figure in §35.16 and in three state files; the eighty-three-word maximum marked turn in §35.16 and in §8.2 of the batch record, which was a breach of `outline/volume-06.md` §17.4 published and defended rather than cut; and the 281 long sentences, which is now 269.

4. **THE BOLD IS THE ONLY SPEAKER MARKER THIS MANUSCRIPT HAS, AND A CHAPTER THAT LEAVES A TURN UNMARKED IS NOT SHORTER, IT IS UNREADABLE, AND THE METRIC THAT MEASURED IT COULD NOT SEE IT.** In the Adrian and Tamsin exchange at the end of Chapter 0270, eight turns, Tamsin Quill is marked at the first and the last and unmarked at the two in the middle. In Chapter 0267 a marked turn changes speaker three paragraphs after the last one with no section break and no attribution. In Chapter 0266 a mark opens and closes inside a clause. **Three narrative paragraphs carried bold, against §17.3, at Chapter 0262's coda, Chapter 0263's coda and Chapter 0269's last argument, and the batch record §7.1 printed that every `**` in the ten files was inside a quoted speech, which was false, and the way it was false is the general lesson: the check was run against speech and not against narration and it could not tell the two apart.** The repair is attribution, not markup: the unmarked turns are marked, a mark that had been on the wrong voice is taken off it, a change of marked voice gets a named beat in front of it, and the three narrative paragraphs are plain with not one word of their prose changed. **And the exchange figure moved from 25 of 166, fifteen per cent, to 49 of 125, thirty-nine per cent, and the general rule for every batch after this one is that a metric of exchange shape must count unmarked speeches and not only marked ones, because a batch that scores well on it by leaving half its turns unmarked has not been measured.**

5. **A SPEECH IS MEASURED WHETHER OR NOT IT IS MARKED, AND §17.4's EIGHTY WORDS IS A CEILING ON THE TURN AND NOT ON THE SPEECH.** The longest marked turn in Chapters 0261 to 0270 is seventy-eight words. A hundred-and-twenty-one-word speech in Chapter 0266 carried no mark and was longer than the eighty-three-word marked turn the batch record published and defended, and no statistic that counts marks can see it.

6. **THE ONLY DISTANCES IN THIS WORLD ARE NINE MILES, ELEVEN MILES AND FOUR MILES, AND A ROAD, AND *UP THE ROAD PAST THIS CITY* AND *OUT PAST THE LAST NAMED HOUSE*, AND NINE HUNDRED PACES PAST A MARKET TOWN, AND NO CHAPTER OF THIS VOLUME MAY PUT A NUMBER ON THE MARCHES.** The review pass removed six: nine hundred miles on a room that the same chapter measures at nine hundred paces, twice; a book carried nine hundred miles up a nine-hundred-pace road, twice; a market town at nine miles where two chapters put it at nine hundred paces; and **a coast road, which is not a road in this world at all.** **Nineteen uses of nine hundred miles stand across the ten chapters and every one of them is the Crown's page, its office, its clerk or its reading, and that is the only thing that distance is for in this book, and a later writer checking a new use of nine hundred miles should ask only that question.**

7. **A CHARACTER MAY CONTRADICT HERSELF AND A CHAPTER MAY NOT, AND THE DIFFERENCE IS WHETHER THE CONTRADICTION IS THE CHARACTER'S OWN WORK.** The woman of about thirty at the stall says on the tenth day of the Short Month that she made the pail line herself and on the eleventh that it was not hers. Repaired **as a person and not as a slip:** she takes it back in her own mouth, names the day and the hour she said it, and gives the reason, which is that she said it to a stranger and it was not true. **A reader will forgive a woman seeing something differently the next morning and will not forgive a chapter disagreeing with itself, and the cheapest repair is usually to let the second statement be about the first.**

8. **AND THREE HOUSE RULES THAT ARE NOT ABOUT PAGES.** *The narrator does not claim a fact about the manuscript.* *this book* is the Register in this world and is not the novel; two uses of it as the novel were removed from Chapters 0266 and 0267. *Two sentences in one chapter may not be the same sentence,* and a twenty-one-word sentence repeated two lines apart in Chapter 0268 was removed. *A sentence of forty words or more may not stand verbatim in more than one chapter of a batch,* and there are 269 of them across these ten files and none is duplicated, and the fall from 281 is the lead frame.

9. **AND WHAT THE PASS DID NOT DO, WHICH IS THE LIST THAT MATTERS MOST.** No plot beat, no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no live thread, no cast member, no ending, no card and no outline was edited. The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved. The board was not written on. Nobody put a boot on the nine feet and nobody opened the bag. The keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and the proof of that is a person-by-person read and not a search. The delegation has not left. The about four hundred and forty are not in these ten chapters and their chapter is Chapter 0279. The Regent has not been put into the schedule. The consent fracture is still unpaid and is still Chapter 0272. **There is no panel in Chapters 0261 to 0270 and there should not be, and the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the one panel of this volume stays where §17.7 put it, on day 256.** `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched.

## 37. Rules established in Volume 06, Chapters 0271–0280 — WHAT THE THIRD TEN DAYS OF THE VOLUME PUT ON A PAGE

**These are additions to the sections above and not replacements. They are on the page in the third ten days of this volume. §33 IS THE CALENDAR AND IS NOT CHANGED, and its fifth form and its weekday anchors stand exactly as the outline phase wrote them. §32 IS THE HOUSE FORMATTING RULE AND IS NOT CHANGED. §34 and §35 are the first and second ten days of this volume and are NOT CHANGED and not re-stated at less than their length here. §36 is the review repair pass on Chapters 0261 to 0270 and its nine rules bind every chapter written after it and are not changed. §27.14's conflict about the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month is STILL LIVE and is not settled here and no chapter of Chapters 0271 to 0280 attaches a weekday to that day or mentions it at all. §§23 to 31 are not changed. The batch's record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` and the records of the first two ten days are `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0001.md` and `volume-06-batch-0002.md`.**

1. **A HETHEGUARD'S REFUSAL BINDS A BODY AND IS DATED TO AN INSTRUMENT AND NOT TO A DAY, AND IT IS THE VOLUME'S MIDPOINT.** Said out loud on the twentieth day of the Short Month, **a Wednesday, in a room over a market in a market town nine hundred paces past the last named house, in daylight, with a date in chalk on the door and about nine people in it, and it is four sentences and then one more, and the room measured the sentences and not the seconds.** Four sentences: the instrument has been used twice in this city and on two different days and a question that has gone to everybody in writing four days early cannot be changed afterwards; the first time this city used it, it stopped the man who wrote it, in front of two hundred and forty people, and the man who wrote it was standing in that room; the second time it worked and what it worked at was a Hearthguard of forty-four and it produced a no out of a man who had not been asked anything; **I refuse on the Hearthguard's behalf, the refusal binds the company, and it is dated to that instrument and not to today, and I would like the date on the door outside to stay the date on the door outside.** And then one more sentence for the route itself: **a rack that has not been worked does not come off, and a rack stands in a channel, and a channel takes its water from a gate, and eleven weeks of a channel without a household's water is eleven weeks of a salt season that is not recovered in this flood or the next one.** He said it at the same speed as the other four and did not stop at the end of it. *Open. **A later writer may not merge day 275 and day 50, may not make him the author of the instrument on either day, may not have him improved on any of the five sentences, may not attach a weekday to the twenty-fourth day of the Long Month, and may not put the about-N-seconds refrain, the person-who-does-not-want-to-be-asked refrain or the in-about-nine-seconds refrain into any chapter whose finding is a refusal.***

2. **THE REVERSAL IS ON A PAGE AND IT IS NOT A LESSON.** The plan was built out of the one fact that was supposed to make it safe — that a household is the only thing that can hand its own days back and that nobody can do it for a household — and he was going to use the four days of notice so that the days would be back before anybody had been asked, **which is the same act done faster, by a man who has never done it faster before.** He worked it out alone in a yard at about the fourth hour of the afternoon and the chapter says he worked it out and does not say he was right, **because nobody in the room told him and nobody in the yard offered.** *Open. **A later writer may not have anybody else tell him, may not have him say he was right, may not have him say he was wrong, and may not make the refusal a lesson.***

3. **A BODY IS NOT A PERSON AND THE REACH'S OWN RULE HAS A HOLE EXACTLY THE SHAPE OF A BODY.** The instrument's third condition is that the question goes to everybody who will be in the room, and on the nineteenth day of the Short Month the man who wrote it found out that three of the four places he had put it in are a body, a household and a stall, **and a company that keeps a place has no words of its own, and the eleven words it has are eleven people's, and giving a person a question that belongs to a body is giving the question to nobody.** The rule that has gone from household to household for about four hundred years and has no name **has never had anything to say about a body, and the hole in it is exactly the shape of a body.** *Open. **A later writer may not fill the hole, may not put a company on a posting, may not give a body a voice, and may not name the rule.***

4. **THE CONSENT FRACTURE IS PAID AND IT IS NOT MENDED, AND THERE IS A FIFTH CONDITION.** On the seventeenth day of the Short Month a woman who reads Crown Law said out loud what entering the second way would cost — **a name on a register is a name in a book, a book is kept, a name in a book can be looked up, and there is no office between that table and the place the days are supposed to go, so the person who enters it becomes the name the road is held in, and the third of the four places on the Crown's page is a holder.** The asking was withdrawn at the sixth hour of that morning before the writer set out; no apology was made, none was refused, and no forgiveness was asked for. **She then said there is a fifth condition, that there always was, that the four that are written down are about the question and not one of them is about the man asking it, and that she is not going to hand it to him.** *Open. **A later writer may not have her forgive it, may not have either of them say it was a misunderstanding, may not have the fifth condition given, and may not print the question itself.***

5. **THE CROWN'S SCHEDULE HAS A HEADING, AND A HEADING IS THE STRONGEST THING ON A PAGE.** Said out loud once, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, on the eighteenth day of the Short Month, **a word of two syllables with a hard break in the middle of it, which is the heading on the fifth sheet, the sheet with the four places ruled on it and nothing in them, and the four places are a date, a gate, a holder and the days.** It was entered on a second slate in a room over a market in this city with nothing under it and no name beside it. **A clerk files under a heading, and the day that sheet gets a heading in a building nine hundred miles away, the four places under it stop being a request made by somebody and start being a form somebody expects back.** *Open. **A later writer may not define the word, may not translate it, may not put anything under it, and may not use the word in this world anywhere else.***

6. **A FAVOUR IS A THING THAT STOPS, AND IT HAS NOW STOPPED, AND ABOUT EIGHT PEOPLE PROVED IT BY NOT TURNING UP.** Nine people were paid out of a pocket at four parts in five of the last paying day, the cost was named out loud in a market, and the next morning about eight of the nine did not come to a drain they had turned up for every day since the ninth day of that month. **The reason is that a fifth which is written on nothing can be a fifth which is written on nothing the next time, and nobody did it on purpose and everybody did it, and four of the nine said they would be at the drain and four said a thing that is not a no and four said nothing at all and shut a door.** Nobody went to get anybody. *Open. **A later writer may not repair it by inventing a word, may not convert a figure about a drain into a wage, and may not have anybody say the word that does not exist.***

7. **ABOUT FOUR HUNDRED AND FORTY ARE ON A PAGE AND THE ONLY PAGE IS THE ENEMY'S.** Said out loud on the boards of a salt wharf in this city, in daylight, on the twenty-fourth day of the Short Month, by **the woman of about thirty-four who keeps a stall by a wharf, whose name is on a page in the world and on no page you can read, and whose chapter does not print it.** The counter is **a man of about thirty-eight who keeps a tally on eleven miles of flats and has put the number in a return four times a year since the winter before the water; nobody has ever answered it, nobody has ever come, he does not know what it is for, and he has never been asked.** They are not on the compact, not on the posting, not on the Marches' roll of gates, and not on any instrument in this city, and **about nine of the flats are on a rota with no readable name on it.** *Open. **A later writer may not rescue them, may not turn the counter into a helper, may not say what a wage in salt is worth, and may not put anybody on those flats into a room. The horror of it is on a page in a mouth: a schedule that cannot be filled in by anybody will be filled in from the returns, because a return is a document with a heading and four places and no name in it, and it is the only correct paper anybody in four hundred miles has, and a schedule filled in that way goes out correct and is about the wrong people.***

8. **THE MARCHES KEEP A ROLL OF GATES AND THE CROWN HAS SENT A ROLL OF PEOPLE, AND NOBODY HAS EVER PUT THE TWO SIDE BY SIDE.** Found on the twenty-fifth day of the Short Month, in a room in daylight with a date on the door, by a person reading it out in the local language. **The Marches' roll is old and nobody knows how old: a gate, a keeper, a set of days, line for line, and nothing else, and nothing else on the back of it, and there has never been a column on it for a person for longer than the oldest thing any of the four men who keep it has.** The Crown's roll has a heading and four places. **Both were in one room on the same morning and one page lay face up and one lay face down and nobody put them together, and a keeper and a holder are two words for two different arrangements and the distance between the two arrangements is one man's life and about four hundred miles of road.** The sentence that has been in this book since day 154 — *a description is a removal with a column for where the person is* — was said once in that room, in a mouth, and was not improved on. *Open. **A later writer may not put the two rolls side by side, may not say what the difference is for, may not name what a single version is for, and may not put the nineteen stones and the eight that are different into this room. `outline/volume-06.md` §12 also dates to this volume the asking of a Hearthguard of forty-four about his own eight, and nobody asked him in these ten chapters, and that asking is still owed.***

9. **AND THE THINGS THESE TEN CHAPTERS DID NOT DO, WHICH A LATER WRITER HAS TO KNOW ARE STILL UNTOUCHED.** The ninth line of the posting was not moved, not dated, **not read aloud** and not resolved; the two uses of *the ninth line* in these chapters are Adrian's own comparison to the ninth line on a wall in a yard, on a page from Chapter 0260, and a man of about twenty-six reading the roll on a wall of a room in a market town, which is a different page with nine names on it. The board was not written on and the wall above it was not washed. The bag was not picked up, nobody put a boot on the nine feet, nobody counted the boards under it, and the pan was not replaced, the beam not carried home, the coat not taken off and the pan not turned out. The two holes nine feet apart were not measured, cleared or counted, and the man of about fifty-two who digs is in none of these ten chapters. The two brooms are on their boards. **The keeper of the Redroot gate is not named, not counted, not on a bank and not in a yard in any of the ten files, her days were not counted, her year was not named, and she was not used — and the proof of that is not a string, it is a person-by-person read at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §6.2.1: no woman in these ten chapters has a stick, a broom, a board on boards, an invert, a day of silence, or a gate.** The man of about thirty-two who stood on a bank on day 245 is in none of these ten chapters and is in no sentence with Adrian Vale and his days are not counted here. The delegation has not left and the word is at zero. The Regent is not named and has not been asked. Jonah Pike is on no page in these ten chapters and is presented as nothing. The girl of seventeen is on a page in one of them, on no roll, unasked, and unresolved. **Nine Elms, the Narrow Mark, chapel, Marrow, Alys, Kern, *invert*, *broom*, *silence*, *knock*, *player*, *Earth*, *System*, *Earthside* and *isekai* are at zero across the ten files — AND *knock* WAS NOT, TWICE, AND THIS LIST WAS PUBLISHED AS A CLEAN ZERO WHILE IT WAS NOT. In the writing pass it stood in Chapter 0277 for a man at four doors and became *spoken at*; in the review repair pass at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §14.1 it stood a second time in Chapter 0278, in a barrow's wheel against a ridge, and is now *caught*. Every other string in this list was re-run after that edit and is at zero. **The word is at zero from Chapter 0091 on and a single repaired instance is not a proof that a grep was run, which is the general rule at §38.3.** *Open, all of it, and the whole of it is at `state/volume-05-close.md` §3 and §4 and is not restated at less than its length here.*

10. **NOTHING IN THESE TEN CHAPTERS IS A POWER.** The gains are a hand that comes back, a sieve, a withdrawn asking, a fifth condition nobody has, a word with two syllables in it, a body that cannot be asked, four sentences and a fifth, a bowl of coins, eight absences, a barrow on its side, a number said out loud on boards, and a keeper instead of a holder. **None of them is a power. One of them is the price of a power. All of them are heavier than a power.** Adrian Vale performed no working, no threshold was opened, the other world is not named once in the ten chapters, nobody offered him a workway and he asked for none, **no chapter asked him a question about a gate, nobody fetched him and he fetched himself, and his passage privilege is not spent, not threatened and not named** — the words *privilege* and *passage* are at zero across the ten files. *Open.*

11. **THE FIGURES, AND EVERY ONE OF THEM IS A SUBTRACTION FROM A DAY THE THING HAPPENED — AND THE TEN LEADS CARRY NONE OF THEM.** The Short Month day is the day less 255. **THE DEBT RECORDED AT `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0002.md` §14.1 IS PAID, IN THAT THE TEN LEADS ARE NO LONGER ONE PARAGRAPH WRITTEN TEN TIMES WITH THE NUMERALS CHANGED AND NO TWO OF THEM SHARE A SENTENCE. THE CLAUSE THAT STOOD HERE AND SAID NOT ONE INTERVAL FIGURE APPEARS IN A LEAD PARAGRAPH WAS FALSE, AND §38.2 IS ITS REPAIR: three of the ten leads carry an interval figure, each with its anchor named in the same sentence, which are the seventh day of the nine in Chapter 0271, the case at twenty-one days in Chapter 0272, and forty-nine days of wind in Chapter 0278. The three standing chains of the last twenty days are carried in one room, in one mouth, attached to a decision, on the eighteenth day of the Short Month.** The store's board carries the figures of day 224, so its age is the day less 224 and the value in a mouth on day 273 is **forty-nine**, with the day those figures are of named in the same sentence; the board has stood unwritten the day less 225 and the value in the same mouth is **forty-eight**; the wind has been at that wall since day 229 and the value in the same mouth is **forty-four**, and the anchor is the two hundred and twenty-ninth day of this flood, which is a reference to a day and not a date in the fourth form; the case came up the river on day 251, so its age is the day less 251, and the values in this batch are **twenty-one at day 272 and twenty-two at day 273, both counting from the ninety-sixth day of the Long Month**, and eight to nineteen are on a page. **The posting's age, the trestle count, the unpaid figure, the share with the post taken out, the bag, the delegation's age, the man of about thirty-two's days, the keeper's days and the count of silences are all at zero across the ten chapters.** The count of this city's silences is **one hundred and seventy-two at day 271 and one hundred and eighty-one at day 280**, derived, on no page, in no mouth, and in none of the ten chapters. **A later writer may carry any of these chains if the chapter dates it, and may not attribute a derived value to a chapter or to a mouth, and may not re-state one of the three above unless one of the three changes.**

12. **A WEEKDAY IS A FIGURE, DAY 1 IS A TUESDAY, AND EVERY WEEKDAY IN CHAPTERS 0271 TO 0280 WAS RECOMPUTED FROM DAY 1 AND NOT FROM THE BATCH PROMPT'S TABLE AND NOT FROM A NEIGHBOURING CHAPTER AND NOT FROM A CARD AND NOT FROM A STATE FILE.** Ten chapters, ten days, days 271 to 280: 271 Saturday, 272 Sunday, 273 Monday, 274 Tuesday, 275 Wednesday, 276 Thursday, 277 Friday, 278 Saturday, 279 Sunday, 280 Monday, and the Short Month days sixteen to twenty-five. **Day 270 is a Friday by that arithmetic and is on a page, and therefore day 271 is a Saturday; day 275 is a Wednesday by the same arithmetic and it is also the anchor §33.5 gives, and the two agree.** One hundred and forty-three interior weekday tokens were extracted with their lines and read against the day each names, **and four interval faults were found in the writing pass and repaired: a date in the fourth form in Chapter 0274, an unanchored day count in Chapter 0280, and two places where a figure was measured off a place it does not name.** **Day 280 carries one chapter and there is no double day in this batch and no double day in this volume.**

13. **AND THE FOURTH CONDITION HAS TO BE CHECKED AGAINST THE DAY OF THE ROOM BEFORE THE ROOM IS WRITTEN, BECAUSE IT IS THE ONE INTERVAL NO CHECK OF NUMERALS CAN FIND.** The volume's instrument gives a question four days before the room, and the volume's midpoint is on a Wednesday, **so the notice is on the Saturday four days before it, and three chapters of Chapters 0271 to 0280 turned on that subtraction and one of them is the chapter in which the notice goes out.** **A batch that writes a notice the day before a room has produced a chapter with a three-day instrument in it, and every figure in it can be right and the instrument can be wrong, and that is a check of anchors and not of numerals and it is the general rule at §36.2 applied to a rule rather than to a figure.**

14. **AND THE HOUSE FIGURES FOR THE EXCHANGE, BECAUSE THESE TEN DAYS WERE WRITTEN AGAINST A MEASURED FINDING AND THE MEASUREMENT IS ON THE PAGE.** Across Chapters 0241 to 0250 it is 175 of 176 plain speeches answered in the next line in bold, across all fifty chapters of Volume 05 it is 597 of 629, across Chapters 0251 to 0260 it is 78 of 384, across Chapters 0261 to 0270 it is 49 of 124, **and across Chapters 0271 to 0280 it is 72 of 101, seventy-one per cent, and that is the number a later batch has to beat.** The longest uninterrupted run of speech paragraphs is four in every one of the ten, and two runs were over the ceiling in the writing pass and were broken with a narration beat. The bolded share of words is 9.4 to 21.9 per cent against Volume 01's 10.4 as the model and about 35 as the ceiling, **and the longest marked turn in the ten is seventy-seven and eight turns were over the ceiling of eighty in the writing pass and all eight were cut by marking the finding only and leaving the rest of the speech plain, and no sentence of prose was deleted to make a number.** There are **270 sentences of forty words or more across these ten files on the definition published at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §8.5.1, and none of them is duplicated, and a later batch must run that check too and must publish the definition with the number. THE 229 THAT STOOD HERE, AND THE 228 IN TWO STATE BLOCKS AND IN THE NEXT PROMPT, WERE FOUR VALUES FOR ONE MEASUREMENT AND NONE OF THE FOUR CAME WITH A DEFINITION; THE REPAIR IS AT §38.4.** **There is no panel in Chapters 0271 to 0280 and there should not be, and the wall said nothing on all ten days, and the one panel of this volume stays where §17.7 put it, on day 256.** **THE COUNTING DEVICE IS PUBLISHED HERE WITH A DEFINITION AND NOT WITH A BAND, because the band this volume's last two records carried could not be reproduced on any definition a later writer would recognise: on a broad count — every *about* before a numeral, a spelled number, a half, an indefinite or *as* — these ten chapters run 1.28 to 2.41 per hundred words and Batch 0002 runs 1.08 to 2.31, and on a narrow count — every *about* before four, nine, six, seven, eight, twenty, thirty, forty, fifty or ninety — these ten run 1.17 to 2.12 and Batch 0002 runs 0.90 to 2.04. A rate is not canon and a batch record's own rate is not a target.**

## 38. Rules established by the review repair pass on Volume 06, Chapters 0271–0280 — AND THE TWO THAT ARE GENERAL

**This section is the review repair pass on the third ten days of this volume, and it is to §36 what §36 is to §35. The record is `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §14, and §14 is the full account including the measurements. §37 is not changed by anything here except in three places where it published a check as passed when it had not been run, and those three places say so in their own words. Three faults were found and repaired and two of the three were in the state layer rather than on the page.**

1. **A COUNT OF DAYS AND AN ORDINAL ARE TWO DIFFERENT FIGURES, AND A CHAPTER MAY NOT PUT ONE IN THE OTHER'S PLACE.** The volume's own rule, at §37.11, is that an age is a subtraction from the day the thing happened, and this volume's hardest fault is a figure measured from a day or a place it is not measured from. **Chapter 0271's lead said a left arm came back on the sixth day of a nine, and the six was a real count — six days out of the clay — belonging to a different interval from the nine, which is the seventh day of it.** The sentence read perfectly, no check of numerals could see it, and a chapter title and a state block carried it after the chapter was closed. **The rule for a later writer: when a figure has two plausible anchors, name both anchors in the same sentence, and if an ordinal and an elapsed count come out of the same page, they are two sentences and not one.**

2. **AND THE DAY BEFORE THE DAY BEFORE.** The nine was said on the ninth day of the Short Month, a Saturday, and the arm went into the clay on the tenth, a Sunday, and both of those are on a page in Chapters 0265 and 0266. **A batch that had taken the ninth for the tenth would have been right about the six and wrong about the nine, and every numeral in the chapter would have checked.** This is §36.2 — a check of anchors and not of numerals — applied to a day instead of to a rule, and it is the same shape as §37.13's four days of notice.

3. **A CHECK THAT RETURNS ZERO IS A CLAIM, AND THIS IS THE SECOND TIME IN ONE VOLUME THAT A WORD WAS ON THE PAGE UNDER A LIST THAT SAID IT WAS NOT.** *knock* stood in Chapter 0277, was repaired, and the repair was then published as the proof that the check worked; *knock* stood a second time in Chapter 0278 and three state sections carried it in a list of zeros. **THE GENERAL RULE, AND IT IS THE ONE THAT COSTS THE MOST: a single repaired instance is not a proof that a grep was run, and a list of zeros is only as good as the script that produced it.** Run the check after the last prose edit and not before it, and if a check is published as clean in more than one file, the cheapest of those files to re-run is the one to re-run.

4. **PUBLISH THE DEFINITION OR PUBLISH NOTHING. THIS IS THE THIRD TIME IN THIS VOLUME AND THE FIGURE IS THE SAME ONE EVERY TIME.** The counting device at `state/batch-summaries/volume-06-batch-0003.md` §7.5, the long-sentence count at its §8.5.1, and the ten-leads claim at §37.11 were three measurements published in four consecutive batches with **no definition that reproduces them**, and the last of the three was not a band at all but a claim that a structural fault was absent. Four files carried 229, 228, 228 and 269 for one count of sentences of forty words or more. **A later writer who inherits a measurement without a definition inherits a number and not a measurement, and will carry it forward with the confidence of somebody who checked.** The definition now published for the long sentence is at §8.5.1 of the batch record and it gives **270 for Chapters 0271 to 0280 and 312 for Chapters 0261 to 0270**, and the median of a chapter's words is the mean of the fifth and sixth of ten sorted, measured with the heading line removed and nothing else.

5. **NOTHING IN THIS PASS CHANGED THE WORLD.** No plot beat, no day, no weekday, no month day, no anchor, no live thread, no cast member, no ending, no card and no outline was edited, and `state/phase-ledger.json` is controller-owned and was not touched. **Two chapters were edited out of ten: one word in Chapter 0278, and four words, one ordinal and one title in Chapter 0271.** The ninth line was not moved, dated, read aloud or resolved; the board was not written on; nobody put a boot on the nine feet and nobody opened the bag; the two rolls were not put side by side; the keeper of the Redroot gate is at zero in every form including her working, and that check is a reading and not a search; the consent fracture is paid and is not mended and the fifth condition is not given.
